- **Result: both routes give 27 correct and the same 2 noted differences** (AVI early frame; untagged colour). Colour within 1–6 of 255 and level within 0.14 dB on the server route (browser route held to ≤ 12 and ≤ 1.5 dB).
- Complete regression with these changes: 291 unit tests; phase0 54, phase3 27, phase4 19, phase5 33, phase6 35, phase7 31, phase8 20, phase9 12, phase11 12, phase12 15, browser-export 23, editor-interactions 15, owner-ui 7, speed 16, soak 7, accessibility audit clean, parity suite pass, start-offset 4, long media 6, input matrix 3, seek conformance 7 configurations with 0 wrong frames in 1,000 random seeks each.
- Not covered: the editor **preview** of these shapes (it shares the readers now used by the browser export, which is an inference, not a measurement, except for start offsets in 13x/13y); 5.1 down-mix coefficients; HDR; real camera files. Vorbis in Ogg is 2.6 ms early in the browser's reader (measured by the probe; inside the tolerance).

**Checkpoint 14c — the 29 shapes in the editor preview: all three routes agree**
- The input matrix has a fourth check: each project is opened in the editor; for picture files six paused frames (0, 1, 2, 45, 100 and the last) are read from the preview canvas and compared with the reference frames; then one playback is run and the beep onsets on the audio clock are compared with the reference (within one frame).
- Result of the first run, no code change needed: all 29 agree with the references (22 picture files with frames compared, 29 playbacks on the audio clock, one file with no sound). The AVI file shows the same early-frame difference as on the export routes and is reported as noted. Checked in the evidence that the measurements are real: e.g. MPEG-TS frames 0,0,1,…,119 and onsets 0.021…3.021 s; MP3 onsets exactly 0, 1, 2, 3 s; the negative-start file 0, 0.9, 1.9, 2.9 s.
- **So for these 29 synthetic shapes the server export, the browser export and the editor preview show the same frames and place sound at the same times** — the first time this is measured rather than argued from shared code. `pnpm conformance:inputs` now has 4 checks and takes about ten minutes.
- After all the sound-clock changes of 13y–14b, the 10-minute A/V-sync export was run again (`pnpm conformance:avsync`): 563 flashes and 563 beeps detected, largest picture-to-sound offset 0.063 ms, no drift between the first and last third.
- Not measured in the preview: colour and level (measured on both export routes only); frames *during* playback (paused frames only), which for late-starting pictures rests on inspecting the proxy file in 13y; anything beyond these 4-second synthetic files.

**Checkpoint 14d — the editor says what it did with a file**
- `assetNotices(asset)` (core) derives, from the asset alone: *converted at import* (with the reasons: rotation, variable frame rate, codec, non-square pixels), *picture starts late* (two frames or more), *sound offset* (50 ms or more, early or late), and *colour encoding not stated* (the "noted" case of the matrix; ingest now records it). The library shows them as text under the file; project summaries for agents (`get_summary`, the summary resource) carry the same list.
- Verified: `notices.test.ts` (2), two ingest tests in `conform.test.ts` (untagged marked, tagged not), and the matrix's preview check now also requires the library to show exactly the expected notices: 4 of the 29 files show one (sound offset; converted ×2; untagged colour), 25 show none. 294 unit tests; phase0 54, phase3 27, phase5 33, accessibility audit clean, input matrix 4/4.
- Limits: informational only — the user cannot choose a colour reading for untagged files; a conformed copy of an untagged source is not flagged although its conversion also assumed BT.601; no notice for the AVI early-frame difference, for beat analysis cut at 20 minutes (stated in the analysis result instead), or for assets imported before this change.

**Checkpoint 14e — still-image matrix: 24 ways of storing one picture; four defects fixed**
- `pnpm conformance:images` (`packages/evals/src/image-matrix.ts`, evidence `docs/evidence/image-matrix.json`): the same four-quadrant picture stored as PNG (RGB, with transparency, indexed, greyscale, 16-bit greyscale), JPEG (baseline, progressive, greyscale, CMYK, orientation tags 2, 3, 6, 8, 20 megapixels), WebP (lossy, lossless with transparency, animated), GIF (one frame, animated), BMP, TIFF, AVIF, a portrait PNG, and an SVG containing a script. Each accepted file is checked at four points (six for the portrait picture) on a server-rendered still and on the editor preview; refusals must be typed.
- **First run: 15 of 24 shown correctly. Defects found and fixed:**
  1. **Photos stored sideways with an orientation tag (tags 6 and 8 — the usual case for phone photos) were shown wrong**: the browser turns them when drawing, but the size recorded at import was the stored one, so the picture was fitted to the wrong shape. Import now reads the tag and records the size as shown.
  2. **AVIF was accepted and then could not be rendered** (it was taken for a one-second video the browser reader could not open).
  3. **A GIF with a single frame became a 0.1-second video clip.**
  4. **BMP and TIFF were refused** (typed, but needlessly). Items 2–4: such stills are now converted to a lossless PNG copy at import, the original bytes are kept, and the library says so.
  5. An animated WebP was refused with a misleading reason ("dimensions missing or exceed 64 megapixels"); the message now says animated WebP is not supported.
- (One flag in the first run was my fixture's mistake — the 16-bit PNG was built from the red channel — not a product fault.)
- **Result: 22 shown correctly on both the rendered still and the preview; 2 refused as intended** (animated WebP; SVG, which is never accepted). Converted pictures show the "converted" notice; the others show none.
- Verified with these changes: 296 unit tests (`conform.test.ts` gained still conversion and orientation-tag tests); image matrix 3/3; input matrix 4/4; start-offset 4; phase0 54, phase3 27, phase4 19, phase6 35, phase12 15; accessibility audit clean. Other proofs were not re-run for this change (it touches still-image import only).
- Not checked: colour profiles (ICC), wide gamut and HDR, CMYK beyond "looks like the RGB original at four points", EXIF orientation in formats other than JPEG, HEIC (the path exists; no HEIC fixture could be made here), animated pictures beyond their first frame, real photographs.

**Checkpoint 14f — subtitle and transcript files in legacy encodings**
- The parser was already well covered (byte-order mark, line endings, tags, bad timestamps). The gap was upstream of it: the editor read every subtitle or transcript file as UTF-8, so a Hebrew file saved as Windows-1255 or UTF-16 — common for older subtitle files — would have been imported as garbage, silently.
- `apps/web/src/textDecode.ts`: a byte-order mark decides; then unmarked UTF-16 (alternating zero bytes); then strict UTF-8; then a legacy single-byte encoding — Windows-1255 when the high bytes form Hebrew runs, otherwise Windows-1252. When it had to guess, the editor says which encoding it used, next to the import button.
- Verified: `textDecode.test.ts` (5: UTF-8 with and without mark, UTF-16 both orders marked and unmarked, Windows-1255 Hebrew, accented Latin not mistaken for Hebrew, empty file) and one end-to-end check in `pnpm proof:editor-interactions` (now 16; four consecutive runs): a Windows-1255 Hebrew SRT imports with its text intact and the note shown; a marked UTF-16 file imports without the note. 301 unit tests; phase6 35, phase12 15, accessibility audit clean.
- The tests caught two flaws in the first version of the detector before it shipped: unmarked UTF-16 Latin text is also valid UTF-8 (so it must be tested first), and a short accented Latin line looked "mostly Hebrew" by byte ratio alone.
- Limits: only these five encodings (no ISO-8859-8 visual-order Hebrew, no Arabic or Cyrillic code pages — those files would be read as Windows-1252 and look wrong, with the note shown); detection is a heuristic and the user cannot override it; imports through REST/MCP take text, so the caller decodes.

**Checkpoint 14g — project-settings matrix: no defect found**
- `pnpm conformance:projects` (`packages/evals/src/project-matrix.ts`, evidence `docs/evidence/project-matrix.json`): one 30 fps source in 15 projects — frame rates 12, 23.976, 24, 25, 29.97, 30, 50, 59.94, 60; sizes 160×90, 640×360, 1280×720, 1920×1080, 1080×1920, 1080×1080, and an odd 641×361 — exported through the server route.
- Result of the first run, no code change: all 15 export with the exact rate in the file (e.g. 24000/1001), the expected number of frames (95 at 23.976, 119 at 29.97, 239 at 59.94), every frame showing source frame floor(i/fps × 30) as read from its barcode, beeps at 0, 1, 2, 3 s, and picture and sound of equal length. The odd-sized project is exported at the next even size (642×362).
- Extended the same day to the **browser export and the editor preview** for seven of the settings (23.976, 25, 29.97, 59.94, 12 fps, vertical 1080×1920, odd 641×361): the browser export has the right frame count, average rate, every frame and the beeps; the preview shows the right source frame at seven paused positions and plays the beeps on time. `pnpm conformance:projects` is now 2 checks. (The first run of this extension flagged the preview; the cause was my test clicking the ruler half a frame too far, not the product.)
- This is a measurement, not a fix: it is recorded because project frame rates other than 30 had never been exported and checked. Checked in the evidence that the numbers are real rather than vacuous.
- Not covered: the preview and the browser export at the other eight settings; other source rates inside these projects (the seek conformance covers 25, 29.97 and 60 fps sources in a 30 fps project only); captions, keyframes and effects at non-integer rates; timecode display at 29.97 (non-drop only); long timelines.
- Complete regression before this run (after checkpoints 14d–14f): 301 unit tests; phase0 54, phase3 27, phase4 19, phase5 33, phase6 35, phase7 31, phase8 20, phase9 12, phase11 12, phase12 15, browser-export 23, editor-interactions 16, owner-ui 7, speed 16, soak 7, accessibility audit clean, parity pass, start-offset 4, long media 6, image matrix 3, input matrix 4, seek conformance 7 configurations with 0 wrong frames.

**Checkpoint 14h — fuzz of all editing operations at five frame rates; an undo defect fixed**
- Until now only the six original operations were tested with random sequences, at 30 fps. `packages/evals/src/ops-fuzz.test.ts` draws random sequences from 45 operations (clips, speed, freeze frame, range cuts, fades, crossfades, volume keys, keyframes, effects and their keys, blend, mask, transitions, markers, formats and overrides, captions and cues, scene splits, transcript cuts, track sound, ducking, loudness, analysis edits) at 30, 25, 23.976, 29.97 and 60 fps, on a project with four assets (including one whose picture starts late) plus analysis data and a transcript.
- Oracles after every step: an accepted step yields a valid, schema-conforming document and does not mutate its input; a refusal is a typed error that changes nothing; the document can be evaluated (picture graph at three frames, audio plan with sane lengths and monotonic envelopes, timeline and summary queries, every linked format projected and validated); and the step's entity changes applied backwards restore the previous document exactly.
- **Defect found on the first run (47 of 60 seeds): undoing the first volume or pan change on a track did not undo it.** Restoring a track copied the old values over it but never removed a setting that did not exist before, so the sound settings stayed. Fixed in `restoreEntities`; a direct regression test is in `entities.test.ts`.
- After the fix: the default run (60 seeds × 70 steps, part of `pnpm test`) passes with 1,870 accepted and 2,330 refused steps, and a one-off larger run (600 seeds × 120 steps) passed with 35,936 accepted and 36,064 refused steps; every operation in the fuzz was accepted at least once. The generator was later re-aimed (per-operation acceptance showed audio crossfade accepted once in 96 attempts, effect edits about 6%); after that the default run has 2,086 accepted steps (crossfade 10 of 94, effect keyframes 22 of 90, freeze frame 28 of 75) and the larger runs passed again: core 38,467 accepted steps, store 7,187 committed steps. 363 unit tests; phase3 27, phase5 33, phase8 20, soak 7, editor-interactions 16.
- **Store level** (`packages/evals/src/store-fuzz.test.ts`, same generator): the random operations are committed through the real store by one actor; then every step is undone in turn, redone in turn, and the whole operation log is replayed from revision 0 — each must reproduce the recorded documents exactly. Default 25 seeds × 40 steps (326 committed steps) in `pnpm test`; a one-off 200 × 80 run passed with 6,858 committed steps. No defect found at this layer.
- What the fuzz does not do: it checks structure and usability, not pictures or sound (no rendering); at the server it covers one actor's commit, undo, redo and replay, not grants, conflicts, proposals or concurrent actors; operation arguments come from hand-written generators, so argument combinations outside them are unexplored; imports (subtitle/transcript text) and recipes are not in it.

**Checkpoint 14i — render fuzz: random projects must render**
- `pnpm conformance:render-fuzz` (`packages/evals/src/render-fuzz.ts`, evidence `docs/evidence/render-fuzz.json`): the same random generator drives real editing sessions on a live server with real media (a video with sound, a sound file, a still picture, a video whose picture starts late), at 30, 25, 23.976, 29.97 and 60 fps. Each resulting project must then render: stills at the first, a middle and the last frame; a picture-and-sound export that succeeds, decodes without errors, has exactly the timeline's frame count and the project's rate, with picture and sound of equal length; a sound-only export; a preflight report; a still for every linked format.
- Result: a first run with 45-step sessions passed but left thin projects (2–11 clips, hardly any effects), so the default is 10 sessions of 160 steps, the generator was re-aimed at the situations hard-to-hit operations need (touching clips, existing effects, the analysed asset), and a fixed "dressing" step before rendering tries a transition on every picture clip that touches its predecessor and adds effects, masks and blend modes. In the latest run all 10 rendered: 4–52 clips, 283–654 frames; speed changes in 7 sessions, freeze frames in 4, effects in 8, masks in 5, blend modes in 6, captions in 9, titles in 9, linked formats in 3, transitions in 2 (3 transitions in all). No defect found; no server error during 1,600 random transaction attempts (855 accepted, 745 refused with typed errors).
- **What it does not show:** that the pictures or the sound are the *right* ones — only that rendering succeeds with the right shape. Correctness of content is covered by the fixed proofs and matrices, on far simpler projects. Transitions remain thinly represented (3 in the latest run): they need touching clips with spare source, which random sessions seldom leave.

**Checkpoint 14j — accessibility audit extended to dialogs and transient states**
- The heuristic audit (`pnpm audit:a11y`) used to inspect only the resting editor; a focus bug in the project dialog had slipped past it for that reason. It now also audits, in Hebrew and English: the marker bar (playhead on a marker), a linked format selected (format badge, safe-area guide, the two "edited for this format only" notes), the project manager dialog and the command palette. Each state gets the same checks (accessible names, contrast, tab semantics, visible focus on 14 Tab steps); the two dialogs are also checked for keyboard behaviour: focus starts inside, 25 Tab and 6 Shift+Tab presses never leave the dialog, it stays open until Escape, Escape closes it, and focus returns to the control that opened it.
- Result: no findings in any state in either language. (A first version of the extension reported "focus not returned" for both dialogs; the cause was the audit itself clicking the page corner — outside the dialog — before tabbing, which closed it. The audit no longer clicks outside a modal.)
- Limits, unchanged in kind: a heuristic, not a WCAG conformance claim; no screen reader; the dialog-behaviour checks have no planted-defect self-test (the name/contrast/focus-ring checks do); not covered: the delete-confirmation sub-form, the shortcuts view of the palette, error and busy states, the access panel's token display, drag interactions.

**Checkpoint 14k — render fuzz: preview, still and export agree on random projects**
- `pnpm conformance:render-fuzz` now has a second check. For each rendered fuzz project it samples frame positions, keeps up to three whose server still is not black (mean level > 0.6 of 255, so titles on black count), and compares the paused editor preview canvas with the still (must be ≤ 0.5 mean difference) and the exported frame (`quality: high`) with the still (≤ 6).
- Result on this profile (10 seeds × 160 steps): 2/2. 24 frames compared in 8 of 10 projects; preview equal to the still on every one (0 mean, 0 worst); export within 0.01–1.89. Two projects had no non-black frame at the sampled positions and contributed nothing. An earlier run with a picture threshold of 8 compared 21 frames in 7 projects with the same outcome.
- This is agreement between the three routes, not a comparison with an independent reference: a mistake shared by all three would pass. Transitions and effects remain thinly represented by the generator.

**Checkpoint 14l — automatic backups with retention**
- `PF_BACKUP_DIR` (off by default), `PF_BACKUP_EVERY_HOURS` (24), `PF_BACKUP_KEEP` (14). `BackupScheduler` in `apps/server/src/backup.ts` runs the existing online backup on an interval and keeps dated database snapshots (`snapshots/pulseframe-<UTC>.sqlite`), pruning to the newest N. Media in the backup is never pruned, so each retained snapshot stays restorable.
- `GET /api/v1/backup` (status), `POST /api/v1/backup` (owner session only; 409 when not configured; 500 with the status when the run failed). Projects dialog shows the schedule, last/next run, snapshots present, the last error, and "Back up now".
- Verified here: 4 unit tests in `backup.test.ts` (retention 5 runs → 3 kept with the right contents; the oldest retained snapshot starts as a working server at its revision; scheduler first run, owner-only, a failing destination is reported and the server keeps serving, recovery, restart waits for the interval, off unless configured); `pnpm proof:owner-ui` 9/8; a11y audit clean; 391 unit tests.
- Not verified: the timer over real hours (only the immediate first run and the computed next time); behaviour when the destination disk fills mid-copy beyond "the run is recorded as failed"; the backup is not read back after writing.

**Checkpoint 14m — shuttle keys (J / L)**
- `Player.shuttle(rate)` (`apps/web/src/player.ts`): moves through the timeline at ±1, 2, 4, 8 times real time on the monotonic clock, skipping frames it cannot draw in time, stopping at either end. Silent: sound plays only at normal forward speed. Commands `transport.shuttleForward` (L: play, then 2×, 4×, 8×) and `transport.shuttleBack` (J: 1×, 2×, 4×, 8× backwards); Space stops. The transport bar shows the direction and speed. K stays "add keyframe" (the usual K = stop is not bound; both are rebindable in the shortcuts dialog).
- Verified here: `pnpm proof:editor-interactions` 24/17 (new check: measured 2.02× forwards and −0.99× backwards on a 12-second timeline, indicator text, Space holds the frame, backwards stops at frame 0), run four times; `pnpm proof:phase3` 52/27 after regenerating the command palette baseline (two new rows); accessibility audit clean; 391 unit tests.
- One earlier run of the new check failed with the forward shuttle not advancing for a second. Probable cause, not proven: the page received the preceding undo after the check started, and a project change arriving during playback pauses it (existing behaviour for normal playback too). The check now waits for the page first and passed four times since.
- Not done: sound while shuttling, speeds below 1×, frame-accurate display at 4× and 8× on this machine (frames are skipped by design; not measured).

**Checkpoint 14n — playback continues when the project changes**
- Before: any project change reaching the editor during playback (the user's own edit, an agent's edit, an undo) stopped playback. Now `Player.setDoc` stops the old schedule and continues from the same frame at the same speed (normal or shuttle), including when the change arrives while playback is still waiting for the audio device. A change while paused does not start playback. Sound restarts from the current frame, so a short gap at the moment of the change is expected; its length was not measured.
- Verified here: `pnpm proof:editor-interactions` 24/18 twice (new check: an agent adds a marker during normal playback and during 2× shuttle; measured speeds 0.98–1.01 and 2.02–2.03 afterwards; a change while paused leaves the playhead at frame 0).
- Regression after this change, all on the current profile: `pnpm proof` 55 · phase3 27 · phase4 19 · phase5 33 · phase6 35 · phase7 31 · phase8 20 · phase9 12 · phase11 12 · phase12 15 · browser-export 23 · owner-ui 8 · speed 16 · soak 7 · parity pass · start-offset 4. Not rerun: seek, long, inputs, images, projects, render-fuzz (they do not play back in the editor), a11y audit (run before this change).

**Checkpoint 14o — marquee selection in the timeline**
- `apps/web/src/editor/TimelineMarquee.tsx`: dragging on empty timeline space draws a box and selects every clip it touches, live; Shift/Ctrl/⌘ adds to the selection; Escape restores the selection from before the drag; a plain click on empty space clears it. Clips on locked tracks are skipped in code. Selection stays session state (no project revision).
- Found while proving it: with text selected, the browser started a native drag and cancelled the pointer, so a later marquee never started. The handler now prevents the default, clears any text selection, and the timeline is `user-select: none`.
- Verified here: `pnpm proof:editor-interactions` 24/19 twice (new check covers box, live selection, add, Escape, clear, unchanged revision); phase3 27, phase5 33, speed 16, owner-ui 8; accessibility audit clean; 391 unit tests.
- Not verified: locked-track exclusion (no check drives it), touch input, a drag that scrolls the timeline. Marquee is pointer-only; keyboard users have Ctrl+A, Alt+↑/↓ and Shift/Ctrl+click on focused clips.

**Checkpoint 14p — title wording and text style per format**
- `set_variant_override` accepts `text` and `textStyle` for title clips (null clears each); stored in the variant's override, applied by `projectVariant`, so preview, stills and export of that format use it and the master and other formats do not. Master edits to fields that are not overridden still reach the format.
- Editor: while a format is viewed, the title's text field and size slider show and edit that format's values, with a note and "back to the master text". Font and other style fields still edit the master from that view.
- Verified here: `variants.test.ts` (new test: isolation, merge, master edits, clearing, refusals); operations and store fuzz include the new arguments (94 tests in those three files); 392 unit tests; `pnpm proof:editor-interactions` 24/20 twice (rendered frame of the format changes; master and another format byte-identical; restore byte-identical); phase12 15, phase3 27, phase5 33; accessibility audit clean.
- Not done: per-format caption text or line breaks; a rendered check of the size override (only the wording is checked on rendered frames). `conformance:render-fuzz` was rerun afterwards with the widened generator: 2/2 (10 of 10 sessions rendered; 24 frames compared).

**Checkpoint 14q — `Secure` session cookie option**
- `PF_SECURE_COOKIE=1` adds `Secure` to the owner session cookie, for use behind a TLS-terminating proxy together with the public origin in `PF_EXTRA_ORIGINS`. Off by default (loopback HTTP).
- Verified here: `apps/server/src/session.test.ts` (attributes with and without the option; session issued to a listed https origin, refused to an unlisted one; the cookie is refused from another origin). No proxy deployment was run; the server still binds to loopback only.

**Checkpoint 14r — request budget per agent grant**
- A grant can carry `requestsPerMinute` (REST and MCP together); when set it replaces the server-wide `PF_AGENT_RPM` for that credential, and it applies even when the server-wide limit is off. Stored with the grant's other constraints (no schema migration; older grants read as "no own budget").
- Verified here: `ratelimit.test.ts` (new test: own budget enforced and refilled on a server with no global limit; a grant without one is unlimited there; a larger own budget is not held to the global 60/min; 0 refused). 394 unit tests.
- The editor's grant form has an optional "requests per minute" field and the list shows the limit. Verified: `pnpm proof:owner-ui` 9/9 (a grant created with 12 is answered 429 on the third request in a row), phase5 33, accessibility audit clean.
- Not done: export quotas per grant.

**Checkpoint 14s — sound longer than the picture**
- **Defect found and fixed:** a file whose sound runs on after its last picture was imported at the picture's length, so the rest of the sound was silently dropped (a 2 s picture with 4 s of sound lost its last two beeps on every route). `apps/server/src/ingest.ts` now makes the asset as long as its sound, in whole frames, when the sound ends a whole frame or more after the picture; the last picture is held under it, as players do. Files whose sound ends with (or before) the picture are unchanged.
- Found by a new input-matrix case, "picture 2 s shorter than sound". Input matrix now: 30 shapes, 4/4 checks, 28 export correctly and 2 noted (the same two as before: the AVI early frame and the untagged-colour file), on the server export, the browser export and the editor preview.
- Verified here after the change: 395 unit tests (new ingest test in `conform.test.ts`); `pnpm proof` 55 · phase3 27 · phase4 19 · phase5 33 · phase6 35 · phase7 31 · phase8 20 · phase9 12 · phase11 12 · phase12 15 · browser-export 23 · editor-interactions 20 · owner-ui 9 · speed 16 · soak 7 · start-offset 4 · projects 2 · long 6 · parity pass. Not rerun: seek, images, render-fuzz, a11y audit.
- Consequence to know: an ordinary file whose sound is a frame or more longer than its picture (common in phone recordings) is now that much longer on the timeline, with the last picture held. Existing projects keep the lengths they were imported with.
- `PF_MATRIX_ONLY=<regex>` runs matching input-matrix cases only (development aid; the count checks then fail by design).

**Checkpoint 14t — backups are read back**
- After the database copy is written, `backupDataDir` opens it, runs SQLite's integrity check and counts the projects; a copy that fails is a failed backup (reported by the scheduler and the CLI). Each copied media file's size is compared with its source before it is moved into place.
- Verified here: `backup.test.ts` 4/4 (the result now carries the project count read from the copy). Later addition: every newly copied media file is hashed and must match the content hash it is named by, and copying no longer blocks the server (asynchronous copy and hashing); a test damages a stored file and the backup fails without keeping the bad copy. Still untested: a damaged database copy, a short file copy. Files already in the backup from earlier runs are not re-hashed.

**Checkpoint 14u — approvals list in the editor**
- The review panel lists requests waiting for the owner (today: an agent's model-download request) with who asked, the capability, the download size, the files and the expiry, and Approve / Reject buttons. It refreshes on the server's `approval` event (now also sent when a request is decided). The review tab's badge counts them.
- Verified here: `pnpm proof:phase5` 34/34 (new check: the request appears with "423.9 MB", rejecting it in the editor closes it and no download job exists); accessibility audit clean; 395 unit tests. Screenshot `docs/evidence/phase5-approval-request-he.png`.
- Not verified: the Approve button in the editor (it would start a real 424 MB download; the approve route itself is covered over REST by the earlier G1 trial). Expired requests leave the list only on the next refresh.

**Checkpoint 14v — source viewer**
- `apps/web/src/editor/SourceViewer.tsx`: each video or sound file in the library has "View and pick a range". The dialog shows the file through the same evaluator, compositor and sound path as the timeline (a one-clip document that is never saved), with play, frame steps, a scrub bar, mark in (I), mark out (O) and "add this range to the timeline" (one `add_clip` with `sourceIn` and `duration`; picture and linked sound together). In and out are whole frames of the project's rate.
- Also fixed: editor shortcuts no longer act on the timeline while a modal dialog (projects, source viewer) is open.
- Verified here: `pnpm proof:phase3` 43/28 (new check: frames 0, 30 and 71 identified by barcode in the viewer; a 42-frame range; one transaction; server stills of the timeline show source frames 30 and 71 at the new clip's first and last frame; keys typed in the viewer leave the timeline and revision untouched; focus returns); accessibility audit clean with the viewer as a dialog state in both languages; proof 54, phase5 34, phase7 31, phase12 15, editor-interactions 20, owner-ui 9, speed 16; 395 unit tests.
- Not verified: a sound-only file in the viewer; a file with a late-starting picture; very long files; marking while playing. No insert/overwrite at the playhead: the range always goes to the end of the timeline. No workspaces.

**Checkpoint 14w — ripple insert (`insert_gap`) and "insert at the playhead"**
- New op `insert_gap {at, duration, trackIds?}`: opens a frame-aligned gap; clips at or after the point move later on every track, a clip lying across it is split there (same rules as `split_clip`: halves stay linked pairs, source continues, keyframes shift), markers at or after the point move (when all tracks are included). A locked track makes it fail. Available to the editor, REST and MCP like every op.
- Source viewer: "Insert at the playhead" commits `insert_gap` + `add_clip` as one transaction; "Add" still appends at the end.
- Verified here: `insertgap.test.ts` (3: split/shift/link/source continuity/keyframes/markers; insert + one undo + closing with `cut_range`; refusals); the op is in the operations and store fuzz (90 tests in those files pass, including undo); 398 unit tests; `pnpm proof:phase3` 43/29 (new check: timeline rows and server stills show …39, then source 100–119, then 40… and the last frame 71; timeline 20 frames longer; one revision); `pnpm proof` 54; accessibility audit clean.
- Not done: overwrite (replace instead of push); insert onto chosen tracks from the editor. `conformance:render-fuzz` was rerun afterwards with `insert_gap` (and the marker change) in the generator: 2/2, 10 of 10 sessions rendered, 28 frames compared in 10 projects.

**Checkpoint 14x — markers under ripple cuts**
- `cut_range` with ripple on every track (and the transcript word cut that uses it) now moves later markers earlier, like `insert_gap` moves them later. Markers on removed frames collapse onto the cut point; only one marker can sit on a frame, so a marker already there wins, otherwise the earliest, and the others are removed and listed in the op output (one undo restores them). Without ripple, or on chosen tracks only, markers stay.
- Verified here: `insertgap.test.ts` (4 tests, including undo of dropped markers); 399 unit tests; phase4 19, phase6 35, phase9 12, phase11 12, editor-interactions 20.
- Behaviour change to know: recipes that remove silences or words now also shift markers; before, markers kept their absolute times.

**Checkpoint 14y — overwrite edit from the source viewer**
- "Overwrite at the playhead": one transaction of `cut_range` without ripple on the first unlocked picture track and the first unlocked sound track, then `add_clip` onto that picture track. Nothing later moves; the timeline keeps its length.
- Verified here: `pnpm proof:phase3` 43/30 (new check: server stills show the frame before, the new 60–69, then the previous 110–119 and the continuation; same length; no overlapping clips; one revision); accessibility audit clean.
- Limits: the target is always the first picture and first sound track (no track targeting); other tracks (titles, captions, upper picture tracks) are left as they are; a sound-only file in the viewer is untested.

**Checkpoint 14z — slip edit**
- New op `slip_clip {clipId, by, includeLinked?}`: the clip keeps its place and length and shows source material `by` later (negative = earlier); linked picture and sound slip together; speed is respected (at 2× ten timeline frames are twenty source frames); refused with the available room when the source has no material that far, and for titles, captions and stills.
- Editor commands: Ctrl/⌘+Alt+←/→ slip by one frame, with Shift by ten (single selected source clip).
- Verified here: `insertgap.test.ts` (5 tests; the slip test covers limits at both ends, picture-only, double speed, refusals); the op is in the operations and store fuzz; 400 unit tests; `pnpm proof:phase3` 43/31 (new check: after 3 + 10 frames the clip shows source 73–82 on server stills, same place and length, neighbours unchanged); editor-interactions 20; speed 16; accessibility audit clean.
- Not done: slipping by dragging; roll and slide as single gestures (both can be composed from two trims in one transaction over REST/MCP); `conformance:render-fuzz` not rerun with the op in the generator.

**Note — a regression run hung once in the Phase 4 proof (unexplained)**
- A full regression started after checkpoint 14z passed `pnpm proof` (54) and phase3 (31), then made no progress inside `pnpm proof:phase4` until the run hit its time limit almost two hours later. Run alone straight afterwards, phase4 passed 19/19 in under seven minutes, and it had passed in the regression after 14s. Cause not found; not reproduced. The regression is being rerun with a time limit per proof so that a hang is reported as such.
- **Regression after checkpoint 14z, rerun in two batches with a time limit per proof: everything passed, no hang.** `pnpm proof` 55 · phase3 31 · phase4 19 · phase5 34 · phase6 35 · phase7 31 · phase8 20 · phase9 12 · phase11 12 · phase12 15 · browser-export 23 · editor-interactions 20 · owner-ui 9 · speed 16 · soak 7 · start-offset 4 · projects 2 (15 settings) · images 3 (22 shown, 2 refused) · long 6 · render-fuzz 2 (with `insert_gap` and `slip_clip` in the generator) · inputs 4 (30 shapes: 28 correct, 2 noted) · parity pass · seek 7 configurations with 0 wrong frames · accessibility audit no findings · 400 unit tests. All on the current CPU profile.

**Checkpoint 15a — caption wording per format (operation level)**
- `set_variant_override` now accepts a manual caption cue: `text` rewords it for one format (its per-word timing is not used there) and `hidden` hides it there. Position and style overrides on a cue are refused with a pointer to the format's caption style.
- Verified here: `variants.test.ts` (new test), 401 unit tests, phase12 15, phase6 35.
- Later in the same checkpoint: the Inspector shows a text field for a selected caption cue. On the master it edits the cue (`update_cue`); with a format selected it stores that format's wording, with "back to the master text". Verified by `pnpm proof:phase12` 19/16 on rendered frames (format frame changes, master byte-identical, restore byte-identical), accessibility audit clean.
- Not done: captions generated from a transcript cannot be reworded per format (their text comes from the transcript); hiding a cue per format has no editor control.

**Checkpoint 15b — close gaps**
- New op `close_gaps {from?, to?}`: removes every stretch of time that is empty on all tracks at once and moves later material earlier (markers follow, as with `cut_range`). A gap on one track under a clip on another is not empty time and stays. Editor: "Close empty gaps in the timeline" in the command palette (no default key).
- Verified here: `insertgap.test.ts` (6 tests; the new one covers two gaps, a track-only gap that must stay, markers, one-step undo, a range, leading empty time, refusals); the op is in the fuzz suites; 402 unit tests; `pnpm proof:phase3` 43/32 (palette command on a real project: afterwards the timeline is exactly as long as its covered frames and no covered frame was lost); accessibility audit clean.
- The operations fuzz now runs 90 steps per seed by default (was 70): with more operations in the generator, 70 steps no longer reached an accepted `delete_effect_keyframe` in the default 60 seeds.

**Checkpoint 15d — frames during playback are checked**
- New check in `pnpm proof:phase3` (now 34): while the editor plays, the barcode is read off the moving preview canvas together with the frame number the player reports, and each sampled frame is compared with the server still of that timeline frame.
- Result of this run: 38 moving frames compared over about four seconds of a timeline with several cuts, 29 different source frames among them, no mismatch. One project, one run, draft-quality playback on this machine: it shows the frames drawn are the right ones, not how many frames are drawn per second and not picture-to-sound timing (that is `conformance:avsync`).

**Checkpoint 15e — reverse playback (picture only)**
- New op `set_clip_reverse {clipId, reverse}` and a clip field `reverse`: a video clip on a video track plays the same stretch of source backwards, in the same place and length. Sound is not reversed: a linked sound clip is unlinked and keeps playing forwards. Refused for freeze frames, stills, sound clips and clips with a transition at either end; `set_transition` refuses next to a reversed clip; `split_at_scenes` refuses a reversed clip. Transcript captions and word cuts ignore reversed clips (they play no speech).
- The frame order is mirrored in one place (`pictureSourceAt`, used by the evaluator, so preview, stills and both export routes agree). Every op that divides or trims a clip goes through `sourceInOfPart`, which knows that a reversed clip shows the END of its stretch first: `trim_clip` (both edges, with the right limits), `split_clip`, `insert_gap`, `cut_range`, `freeze_frame`; `slip_clip` and `set_clip_speed` needed no change.
- Editor: "Play backwards" checkbox in the Inspector with a note about the sound; a ◀ mark on the clip.
- Verified here: `packages/engine/src/reverse.test.ts` (4 tests: for each of those ops, the source frame shown at every remaining timeline frame is what it was; limits at both ends of the source; undo; switching back); the op is in the operations and store fuzz; 406 unit tests; `pnpm proof:phase3` 43/35 (new check on server stills: ten frames show 82 down to 73, neighbours unchanged, paused preview equal, unchecking restores); proof 54, speed 16, phase6 35, phase7 31, phase12 16, editor-interactions 20; accessibility audit clean.
- Added afterwards: the phase3 check also exports the stretch through the server route and reads the barcodes from the file: the same twelve frames in the same order as the stills. Thumbnails on a reversed clip now run in the order the clip plays (checked by eye on one clip, forwards and reversed; no automated check).
- `conformance:render-fuzz` was rerun with the op in its generator and passed 2/2 (23 frames in 8 projects), but its evidence does not show whether a rendered project contained a reversed clip, so it is not counted as evidence for reverse.
- The render fuzz now records which features are on the picture at each compared frame and always includes the middle of up to two clips that are reversed, re-timed or frozen. Run after that change: 2/2, 35 frames in 9 of 10 projects; frames per feature: reverse 4, speed 6, freeze 9, effect 12, mask 8, blend 9, keyframes 9. No compared frame fell inside a transition, so transitions are not covered by this comparison.
- Not done or not verified: reversed sound; transitions next to a reversed clip; how fast a reversed clip plays in the preview (each frame is a backwards seek; not measured); the browser export route with a reversed clip (same evaluator, not run).

**Checkpoint 15f — transitions next to a reversed clip; a flaw found in the render fuzz**
- Transitions are now allowed next to a reversed clip. One helper (`handleAfterEnd`) answers "how long could this clip keep playing past its end": later source for a forward clip, earlier source for a reversed one. `set_transition`, the preflight warning and `set_clip_reverse` (which refuses if the transition into the next clip would lose its spare source in the new direction) use it. The evaluator needed no change: the outgoing reversed clip simply continues backwards.
- Verified here: `reverse.test.ts` (5 tests; the new one checks the outgoing and incoming source frames through a 10-frame transition, the 50/51-frame limit, a transition at the reversed clip's own start, and the refusal when turning forwards would leave too little source); 407 unit tests. No rendered check of such a transition yet.
- **Flaw in my own test, found here:** the render fuzz's "dressing" step asked for four transition types of which three do not exist (`cross-dissolve`, `dip-to-black`, `slide-left`), so those requests were refused and almost no transitions reached the rendered projects. Earlier statements that the render fuzz covered transitions "thinly" were therefore generous: it covered `wipe-left` on a quarter of the eligible cuts at most. The names are corrected (`dissolve`, `dip-black`, `wipe-left`, `push-left`) and frames inside transitions are now always among the compared frames.
- Rerun with the corrected names: 2/2, 39 frames in 9 of 10 projects. Five compared frames lie inside a transition, in 3 projects (one of them on a reversed clip with an effect and a mask): preview equal to the still on all five, export within 2.54 of 255. Transitions still reach only a few projects, because random sessions seldom leave two picture clips touching; this is agreement between routes, not a check against an independent reference.

**Checkpoint 15g — detach picture from sound**
- New op `unlink_clip {clipId}`: removes a clip from its link group (and unlinks the one clip left behind), so picture and sound can be edited separately. Inspector button "Detach picture from sound" on a linked clip.

**Checkpoint 15h — tracks can be locked, hidden, muted, renamed and removed**
- **Gap found:** every part of the system honoured a track's `locked`, `hidden` and `muted` flags, but no operation could set `locked` or `hidden`, rename a track or remove one (only `muted` through `set_track_audio`). Lock and hide were therefore unreachable for users and agents.
- New ops: `update_track {trackId, name?, locked?, hidden?, muted?}` (works on a locked track so it can be unlocked; `hidden` for picture/title/caption tracks, `muted` for sound tracks) and `remove_track` (empty, unlocked, not referenced by ducking or a caption track).
- Editor: each track header has a lock button and a hide (or mute) button, with pressed state and labels; a locked or switched-off track is marked. Commands that change the selected clips are inert while a selected clip is on a locked track.
- Verified here: `reverse.test.ts` "track settings" (hidden picture not drawn, muted sound not mixed, locked refuses edits and ripple edits, unlock, undo, remove and undo, refusals); both ops in the fuzz suites; 409 unit tests; `pnpm proof:phase3` 43/37 (hide removes the picture from the server still and showing restores the same frame; lock refuses a delete over the API and the Delete key does nothing; unlock); visual baselines refreshed; accessibility audit clean.
- Not done: reordering tracks (undo treats track order as ordering metadata, so a pure reorder would not be undoable without a change there); adding a track from the editor (the op exists); rename has no editor control.

**Checkpoint 15i — project frame can be changed; library files can be renamed and removed**
- **Two more gaps of the same kind as 15h:** a project's frame size, background and fill could only be set when the project was created, and a library file could never be renamed or removed.
- New ops: `set_project_format {width?, height?, background?, fit?}` (even sizes; clips keep their relative layout; the frame rate stays fixed), `rename_asset`, `remove_asset` (refused while a clip uses the file; its transcript and analysis go with it; the stored media stays until storage clean-up, so undo restores the entry).
- Editor: "Project frame" form at the top of the Deliver panel; "Remove from library" on each library file (disabled while in use).
- Verified here: `reverse.test.ts` (2 new tests: frame change in the evaluator, layout fractions unchanged, undo, refusals; rename, remove with analysis, undo, refusals); all three in the fuzz suites; 411 unit tests; `pnpm proof:phase12` 19/17 (editor: odd size blocked, 480×480 with the new background colour in the corners of the server still, Ctrl+Z restores, in-use file cannot be removed, unused one is removed); proof 54, phase3 37 (baselines refreshed for the new library button), phase5 34, owner-ui 9, editor-interactions 20; accessibility audit clean.
- `conformance:render-fuzz` rerun with the ops of 15g–15i in the generator: 2/2, 10 of 10 sessions rendered, 44 frames in 9 projects (transition 10, reverse 8, effect 20, mask 16, keyframes 6, blend 5, freeze 4, speed 2).
- Not done: changing frame rate or sample rate of an existing project; rename has no editor control; removing a file does not cancel an analysis job already started for it; the job then fails with a typed error and attaches nothing (`analysisJobs.test.ts`, added afterwards; 412 unit tests).

**Checkpoint 15l — track order**
- New op `move_track {trackId, toIndex}`. Undo needed a new tracked entity, the track order of a sequence, compared only over the tracks both states have, so adding or removing a track is still not an order change and an undo of a move still works after other tracks were added. Restoring it puts the named tracks back in their relative order in the slots they occupy.
- Editor: "Move the selected clip's track up / down" in the command palette.
- Verified here: `reverse.test.ts` "track order" (what is drawn on top changes; the diff is exactly the order entity; undo; undo after an unrelated track was added; refusals); the op is in the operations and store fuzz (the store fuzz undoes and redoes random histories); 413 unit tests; `pnpm proof:phase3` 43/40 (palette command, timeline rows follow, Ctrl+Z restores).
- **Miss of mine, caught here:** `pnpm proof` (Phase 0) had been failing since checkpoint 15j and I had not rerun it after that change. It clicked "the first button" of a library entry, which became the rename button. The product was fine; the proof now clicks the add button by its test id and passes 54/54. A full regression follows.
- A grant limited to certain tracks or a time range cannot reorder tracks (the order change falls outside it), by the existing rule for anything that is not a clip or a granted track.

**Checkpoint 15m — reversed sound; picture and sound reverse together**
- `set_clip_reverse` now also applies to sound clips. A linked picture and its sound reverse together and stay linked (`includeLinked: false` reverses one side and unlinks the pair). The audio plan marks a reversed clip; the three mixers read the same stretch of source and reverse it: the server mixer with ffmpeg (`areverse`, held in memory for the clip's length), the browser export by reversing decoded buffers, the preview by decoding the remaining stretch in two-second windows from its end. Waveforms of reversed clips are drawn mirrored. A crossfade out of a reversed sound clip is refused.
- Verified here: `pnpm proof:reverse-sound` 7/7 (new; one 4-second fixture): server export shows frame 119 − i on all 120 frames and each beep within 3 ms of its mirrored position; a frame range of the reversed clip; the browser export (same frames, beeps within one frame); the editor preview (paused frames by barcode, beeps on the audio clock within one frame); switching back restores the forward export. `apps/server/src/reverseSound.test.ts` (mirrored burst, partial range, double speed). `reverse.test.ts` updated (9 tests).
- Not verified: reverse combined with speed in the two browser routes (server only); long reversed clips (memory in the server mixer grows with clip length); reversed sound across a format variant; how reversed playback sounds at window boundaries (measured only by beep onsets).

**Checkpoint 15n — MCP change notifications**
- The installed MCP server library already supports `subscriptions/listen`; the server now advertises resource subscriptions and publishes `notifications/resources/updated` for a project's summary and timeline on every commit, and `notifications/resources/list_changed` when a project is created or deleted. There is one handler (and event bus) per credential, so a credential limited to certain projects is never told that another project changed, even when it asks for that URI.
- Verified here: `packages/evals/src/mcp-notifications.test.ts` with a real MCP client over HTTP (commit over REST and over MCP both notify; an unsubscribed project does not; a new project triggers list_changed; nothing arrives after closing; a one-project grant hears only its project and no list change).
- Limits: only protocol revision 2026-07-28 clients can listen (older clients poll); notifications say that something changed, not what; handlers are kept per credential for the life of the process.

**Checkpoint 15o — export quota per grant, backup verification, measurements as jobs, full history, wording**
- A grant can carry `exportsPerDay`: exports started in any 24 hours, counted per grant in the database (two grants with the same name are separate), checked before and recorded after a successful start, typed 429 `export_quota_exceeded`. Field in the editor's grant form. Tested in `reverseSound.test.ts` (second test) and `pnpm proof:owner-ui` 9/9.
- `pnpm backup --verify <dir>`: every database in a backup (current and each snapshot) is integrity-checked, every media file is re-hashed against its name, and media the database refers to must be present. Tested in `backup.test.ts` (clean, a flipped byte, a truncated snapshot, a missing file).
- Silence and loudness measurements accept `background: true` and then run as durable jobs whose output is the same object the in-request call returns; the project is not changed. Tested in `analysisJobs.test.ts`.
- "Full history" in the review panel: every transaction, newest first, in the interface language, with a filter for own or agent edits. The activity wording now covers the operations added since 14w (they showed as raw names). `pnpm proof:phase3` 41/41; accessibility audit clean with the dialog as a state.

**Checkpoint 15p — colour reading choice for untagged files**
- For a video file that does not state its colour matrix, the library note now has a choice: read as the browser does (BT.709) or as BT.601. The choice is stored on the asset (`set_asset_colour`) and applied in the compositor as a 3×3 correction before effects, so preview, stills and both export routes agree.
- Verified here: `look.test.ts` (the matrix against independent arithmetic); input matrix, the two untagged cases run alone: without the choice 13 of 255 from the BT.601 reference (noted, as before), with it 5 of 255 and "correct" on the server export, the browser export and the editor preview.
- Cost: the correction reads and writes every pixel of the frame in JavaScript for each frame of such a clip. Its effect on preview speed was not measured.

**Checkpoint 15q — the Phase 4 hang explained and fixed; shutdown no longer waits for open streams**
- The unexplained hang of `pnpm proof:phase4` recurred during a regression and was examined while stuck. Cause: a step in its editor part failed (after "Cancel", the canceled state did not appear within 15 s, during a period when other test suites were also running on the machine); the proof then skipped closing its browser, and the server's `close()` waited for that page's event stream forever.
- **Product defect fixed:** `close()` now drops connections that are still open after it stops accepting. `apps/server/src/shutdown.test.ts` fails without the fix (shutdown did not finish in 5 s with one open event stream) and passes with it. The proof now always closes its browser.
- Still open: why the canceled state took more than 15 s in that run. It has only been seen under heavy parallel load; not reproduced on its own. *(Explained and fixed at checkpoint 16g: a cancel racing the opening of the render page.)*

**Checkpoint 15r — fit music to the picture; analysis marks under reversed clips**
- New op `fit_music {clipId, to?, fadeOut?}`: a sound clip that is not linked to a picture is made to end on the last detected beat at or before the end of the picture and titles (exactly there when the file has no beat analysis) and fades out; when the music is shorter than the picture it ends on its own last beat and the output says how much is missing. Button "End the music with the picture" in the Sound panel.
- Beats, scene cuts and silence ranges are now mapped through reversed clips correctly (they were mapped as if the clip ran forwards).
- Verified here: `insertgap.test.ts` (2 new tests), `reverse.test.ts` (marks under a reversed clip), fuzz suites with the op, `pnpm proof:editor-interactions` 24/21 (the music ends on the last beat before the picture's end, with a fade; undo restores).
- Not done: looping or re-arranging music that is too short; downbeats (bar starts) are not detected, so "last beat" is any beat.

**Checkpoint 15s — speed ramp (stepped)**
- New op `speed_ramp {clipId, from, to, steps}` and a "Speed ramp" form in the Inspector: the clip (and its linked sound) is replaced by consecutive steps, each at a constant speed between the two values, reading the source on without gaps or repeats. A stepped ramp, not a continuous curve: this keeps every time an exact rational (ADR-0003). The first step keeps the clip's id; one undo restores the single clip. Refused on clips with keyframes, volume keys or a transition, on freeze frames, reversed clips and stills, and when the new length would run into the next clip.
- Verified here: `reverse.test.ts` "speed ramp" (2 tests: step lengths, linked pairs, the picture never goes back, each step starts where the last stopped reading, undo, refusals); the op is in the fuzz suites; `pnpm proof:speed` 19/18 (0.5× → 2× in four steps: every exported frame is the one the steps call for, read from its barcode; undo).
- Not done: a continuous ramp; the sound steps in pitch at each boundary (not measured); ramps on clips that carry keyframes.
- A generator bug surfaced and was fixed: the fuzz picked a track after a random session had removed every track.

**Checkpoint 15t — roll edit; repeated key presses no longer lose a step**
- Roll: Ctrl/⌘+[ and Ctrl/⌘+] move the cut between the selected clip and the clip before it by one frame (two trims in one transaction; nothing else moves).
- **Defect found by the roll check and fixed:** pressing a nudge, roll or track-move key twice quickly applied only one step, because the second press was worked out against the document as it was before the first commit landed. These commands now compute their operation when the commit actually runs.
- Verified here: `pnpm proof:phase3` 43/42 (two presses move the cut two frames: the earlier clip plays two frames more, the selected clip starts two frames later, the timeline keeps its length, and two presses back restore it).
- Not done: slide; roll by dragging.

**Checkpoint 15v — beat analysis of long files**
- The beat tracker used to read only the first 20 minutes of a file. It now analyses a longer file in consecutive 20-minute windows, each with its own tempo estimate, up to four hours; the stored result says how many windows were used (and, beyond four hours, that only the first four were analysed).
- Verified here: `beats.test.ts` (a 48-second synthetic loop analysed in 12-second windows: one continuous grid, no doubled beat or hole where windows meet, same tempo); `analysisJobs.test.ts`, `analysis.test.ts`; `pnpm conformance:long` 6/6 (its 20-minute file is exactly one window).
- Not verified: a real file longer than 20 minutes (no multi-window run on real media); music whose tempo changes inside a window is still tracked with one tempo for that window.

**Checkpoint 15w — the AVI one-frame difference is fixed**
- **Defect fixed:** files converted at import (variable frame rate, rotation, unsupported codec, non-square pixels) were brought to a constant rate by the encoder option `-fps_mode cfr`, which fills a gap in the timestamps with the frame that FOLLOWS it. The AVI test file has such a gap after its first frame, so the copy showed frame 1 one frame early (0, 1, 1, 2 instead of 0, 0, 1, 2). The conversion now uses the `fps` filter, which holds the previous frame, as players do.
- Verified here: input matrix 4/4 on 31 shapes: 30 correct and 1 noted (the untagged-colour file when no reading is chosen; the same file with the BT.601 choice is correct), on the server export, the browser export and the editor preview; `conform.test.ts` 12/12.
- Existing projects keep the copies made at their import; only new imports get the corrected conversion.

**Checkpoint 15x — pruning a backup**
- `pnpm backup --prune <dir>` reports media in a backup that no database in it lists (current or any retained snapshot); `--apply` removes it. It refuses when a database in the backup cannot be read.
- Verified here: `backup.test.ts` (a stray file is reported, kept without `--apply`, removed with it; the backup still verifies; an unreadable snapshot stops pruning).

**Checkpoint 16a — upload-in-progress and the error bar in the accessibility audit**

**Checkpoint 16b — volume line on the clip**
- A sound clip with volume keys shows its level line on the timeline. When the clip is the selection each key is a handle: drag (up/down = level on a 0–2 scale, sideways = time in whole frames, kept between its neighbours), arrow keys nudge it (Shift = larger level step), Delete or a double-click removes it, a double-click on the line adds a key at the level there. Handles are named buttons (time and percent). All of it goes through the existing `set_volume_keyframe` / `delete_volume_keyframe` ops; a drag is one transaction. A drag that starts on the line still moves the clip.
- **Defect found while proving it and fixed:** two quick arrow presses on a key counted once (computed from the rendered state); the nudge is now computed against the live project when it runs.
- Verified here: `pnpm proof:editor-interactions` 24/23 (new check), `pnpm proof:phase3` 43, `pnpm proof:phase8` 20, accessibility audit clean, 429 unit tests.

Edges of other clips at the cut's own starting time (linked sound, other tracks) do not hold the dragged edge there. The shortcuts view now ends with a line describing the mouse gestures (move, trim, roll, Alt = no snapping).
- K stops playback even when the selected clip is on a locked track (adding a keyframe there is still refused).
- Verified here: `pnpm proof:phase3` 44/44 (new check: the drag gives the same frames as the keyboard roll, one revision, same timeline length, one undo restores it).

**Checkpoint 16g — a canceled export could hang the export queue: found and fixed**
- **Defect (real, intermittent):** canceling an export at the moment its picture render was starting could leave the job in "cancel requested" for good, and every later export waited behind it until the server was restarted. Found because the accessibility audit's new export-in-progress state did not see the cancel finish within 30 s in one of two runs. This is also the explanation of the Phase 4 run that stopped making progress (checkpoint 14z note) and of the canceled state that once took more than 15 s (checkpoint 15k note): both were this, not machine load.
- Cause, shown with a reproduction that cancels at the first "rendering 0/N" (stuck in 1 of 3 and 1 of 12 rounds before the fix): (1) a cancel that arrived while the render page was being opened had nothing to abort yet; (2) when it arrived just after the abort hook was registered, closing the browser context left Playwright's pending `newPage()` call unsettled for ever, and the export waited on it; (3) frames posted to an encoder that had already been killed could wait for ever on a closed pipe.
- Fix: the render worker's wait ends on abort whatever the pending page calls do, closing the context is bounded (a context that will not close drops the browser so the next render starts a fresh one), the exporter aborts at once if the cancel preceded the hook, and frame delivery refuses immediately (409) when the encoder is gone or exits during a write.
- Verified here: the reproduction 40/40 canceled within 121 ms each (each a running job, not a queued one) and an export started afterwards succeeded; `pnpm proof:phase4` 20/20 (new check: cancel at "rendering 0/600" of the 1080p job stops within 5 s); accessibility audit clean again; 430 unit tests.
- The browser-export route was checked afterwards (checkpoint 16r): it has no queue and no render worker on the server, and a cancel the tab never acknowledges ends at lease expiry (15 s) — covered by `browser-export.test.ts`, including a new test that a second export starts while the first is waiting to be canceled. Still not verified: behaviour if Chromium itself is killed during a server render.

**Checkpoint 16h — slide a clip by dragging**
- Ctrl/Cmd + dragging the body of a single clip that has a clip touching it on each side slides it between them (the clip keeps its length and content; the neighbours give and take), as one transaction; each neighbour keeps at least one frame. No snapping during a slide.
- Verified here: `pnpm proof:phase3` 45/45 (new check: the drag gives the same frames as three keyboard slides, one revision, same length, one undo restores it).

**Checkpoint 16k — bar starts (downbeats) in the beat analysis**
- The beat tracker now also reports which beats start a bar, when there is a regular accent: for bars of four and of three beats it finds the position whose beat is the loudest of its bar most often, and reports bars only if that holds in at least 70 % of the bars and those beats are clearly louder; otherwise it reports none rather than guessing. Loudness (the tenth of a second after the beat) is used because the onset strength under an accented and a plain hit turned out nearly equal on the test loops.
- Stored with the beats (`downbeats`, `meter`), each bar start is one of the beats; `edit_analysis` can set or clear them and keeps the ones that are still beats when beats are corrected. Shown as taller markers on the timeline and as a count in the analysis panel; in `get_analysis` (`barCount`, `downbeatSeconds`, `meter`) and the project summary. The stored method text says in what share of bars the accent was found.
- Verified here: `beats.test.ts` (4 new tests: 4/4 at three tempi and start offsets, 3/4, no accent → none, the 70 % rule), `insertgap.test.ts` (new test), `pnpm proof:phase9` 14/14 (two new checks: corpus of three accented loops — right meter, precision 1.0, recall 0.93–1.0 at ±70 ms; editor markers and panel), `pnpm proof:phase5` 34, `pnpm proof:phase11` 12, 436 unit tests.
- **Limits, stated plainly:** verified on synthetic loops only. Real music often accents other beats (back-beat snare on 2 and 4), which this rule would then call bar starts or reject; no bars of 2, 5, 6 or 7; one meter per 20-minute window. `fit_music` uses bar starts from checkpoint 16l; beat-synced cuts still use any beat.

**Checkpoint 16l — fit music ends on a bar start**
- `fit_music` ends the music on the last bar start at or before the end of the picture when the beat analysis has bar starts (output `onBar`), and on the last beat as before when it has none or when the last known bar start lies more than one bar before the last usable beat.
- Verified here: `insertgap.test.ts` (extended: ends at the bar start, falls back to the last beat), `pnpm proof:editor-interactions` 24/23, 436 unit tests. Not verified on real music. Beat-synced cuts use bar starts from checkpoint 16o.

**Checkpoint 16m — keep the pitch when a clip's speed changes**
- New clip setting `keepPitch` (op `set_clip_pitch`; a tick box under the speed field): at a speed other than 1 the sound is stretched or tightened instead of resampled, so its pitch stays. Given a picture clip, it is stored on the linked sound clip. Default stays as before (pitch follows speed).
- One shared stretcher (`packages/engine/src/stretch.ts`, waveform-similarity overlap-add, 40 ms windows, ±12 ms alignment search; pure, streaming, same output whatever sizes the input arrives in) is used by all three mixers. Server and browser export run it from the START of the clip, so a range export is the same sound as that range of the full export; the preview starts it at the playhead, so it can differ from export by a few milliseconds of alignment. Works with reversed clips.
- Verified here: `stretch.test.ts` (7 tests: a 440 Hz tone at 0.5/0.75/1.25/2× keeps pitch within 3 Hz and level within 10 %, no clicks; a click lands at its time ÷ speed within 15 ms; chunk-size independence, bit for bit; channels separate; bounded memory); new `pnpm proof:pitch` 6/6 on the beep fixture — server export at 0.5× (1 kHz kept where plain speed gives 500 Hz; beeps start up to 18 ms early), range = full export sample for sample, browser export, editor preview (beep onsets, tick box), 2× forwards and backwards, refusals and speed 1; `proof:speed` 19, `proof:reverse-sound` 7, `proof:browser-export` 23, `proof:phase8` 20, accessibility audit clean, 443 unit tests.
- **Limits, stated plainly:** verified on tones and beeps only — no speech, no music, nobody has listened to it. One method for everything: a sharp sound gets a pre-echo of up to about 20 ms and strong slow-downs smear drum hits; it is meant for roughly 0.5×–2×. Browser export holds the clip's whole stretch in memory. The preview's frequency was not measured (only beep onsets). The steps of a speed ramp do not carry the setting over a step boundary smoothly (each step is its own clip).
- Also: the operations fuzz had been passing its "every operation accepted at least once" check for `delete_effect_keyframe` by luck of the random sequence; adding an operation shifted the sequence and exposed it. The generator now makes effect keys more often and deletes existing ones.

**Checkpoint 16n — continuous speed ramp**
- New clip field `speedEnd` and op `set_clip_speed_ramp {clipId, from, to}`: the clip stays ONE clip and its speed changes evenly along it from `from` to `to` (the stepped `speed_ramp` stays as the "in steps" variant). It keeps showing the same stretch of source; its length becomes that stretch ÷ the average of the two speeds, down to a whole frame. Linked picture and sound change together; the sound's pitch glides with the speed.
- Time mapping: after τ of the clip, v0·τ + (v1 − v0)·τ²/(2D) of source has played. Computed exactly in integers and rounded down to 1/35,280,000 s (a multiple of the usual frame and sample rates, so times on a frame or sample boundary stay exact). The inverse (source time → timeline, used for caption and marker positions) needs a square root and is approximate to a microsecond; its users snap to frames.
- Sound: one shared variable-rate resampler (`RampResampler` in `packages/engine/src/stretch.ts`, four-point cubic) in the server export, the browser export and the preview; exports run it from the start of the clip, so a range export equals that range of the full export.
- **Deliberate limit:** while a clip has a gradual change it cannot be trimmed, divided (split, ripple cut through it, gap inserted across it, freeze frame), slipped, reversed, given a transition or crossfade, fitted to the picture or set to keep its pitch — each is refused with the reason and the way round (give it one speed with `set_clip_speed`, edit, ramp again). The speed at an arbitrary cut is generally not an exact ratio of small numbers, and rounding it would shift frames. Moving, copying, deleting, effects, keyframes and volume keys work.
- Editor: "Smooth change" and "In steps" buttons in the speed-ramp box, the current ramp with "back to one speed", a ⤴ mark on the clip.
- Verified here: `speed.test.ts` (5 new tests incl. a property test over speed pairs and clip lengths: valid project, never past the source, never backwards; exact values at chosen frames; all refusals), `stretch.test.ts` (2 new: a 440 Hz tone gliding 440 → 880 Hz, level kept; chunk-size independence and starting part-way), the operations and store fuzz with the new op, `pnpm proof:pitch` now 10/10 — ramp 0.5× → 2× on the barcode fixture: all 96 exported frames equal the formula on the server and in the browser export, beeps within 5 ms (server) and at 1 kHz × the speed of that moment within 5 %, range = full export, preview frames and beep onsets, Inspector state and "back to one speed".
- Not done / not verified: no curve other than a straight change of speed (no ease in/out); no low-pass before reading faster than normal, so high frequencies can alias above about 1.5×; transcript captions on a ramped clip use the approximate inverse (not checked in a proof); nobody has listened to it.

**Checkpoint 16o — beat montage can cut on bar starts**
- The beat-montage recipe takes `cutOn: beats | bars`. On bars it cuts on the bar starts of the beat analysis (every N bars) instead of counting beats from the first one in the clip; it refuses with a way forward when the analysis has no bar starts, and offers the other mode as an alternative when it has. The recipe form has the choice.
- Verified here: `recipes.test.ts` (new, 2 tests: the cut frames in both modes, the refusal, the planned operations apply), `pnpm proof:phase11` 12/12, 452 unit tests. The form control itself was not exercised in a browser proof.

One definition (`packages/core/src/volume.ts`) serves the audio plan, the Sound panel and the drawn line; the plan turns a smooth stretch into 16 straight pieces, so the three mixers needed no change.

**Checkpoint 16t — high-dynamic-range sources are tone-mapped at import**
- **Defect fixed:** an HDR file (PQ / HDR10 or HLG, typically 10-bit HEVC from a phone or camera) was converted at import for its codec only, without regard to its transfer function, so its picture came out grey and washed out (the 75 % grey bar of a test clip at about 128 instead of 190). Import now recognises the two HDR transfer functions and tone-maps the editing copy to standard range: HDR reference white (203 nits) becomes standard white, BT.2020 becomes BT.709, and highlights above reference white are rolled off rather than clipped. The asset is marked "converted at import: high dynamic range converted to standard range", and the original bytes are kept as for every conversion.
- Verified here: `conform.test.ts` (new test, PQ and HLG clips made from colour bars: grey within 8 levels (PQ) or 25 (HLG) of its standard value, red, green and blue bars saturated), input matrix 4/4 unchanged, 457 unit tests.
- **Not done, and not possible on this pipeline:** HDR output. The compositor is 8-bit Canvas2D in BT.709; an HDR export needs a higher-bit-depth (GPU) pipeline (B-1). Also not done: Dolby Vision metadata is ignored (the base layer is used); dynamic HDR10+ metadata is ignored; no choice of tone-mapping curve or reference level; verified on synthetic bars only, not on camera or phone footage. Projects imported before this keep their washed-out copies until the file is imported again.

- Sound follows on all three routes (the ramp resampler takes the window and the curve). At a cut inside a ramp the two sides join with a sub-sample shift (each part's source starts on a whole sample).
- Still refused on a ramped clip, each with the reason: slip, reverse, freeze frame, transition, crossfade, fit-to-picture, keep-pitch and the stepped ramp.

- Owner decisions recorded: a local speech-recognition model may be downloaded (up to 5 GB); a local voice model may be downloaded (up to 5 GB); the GPU reference machine is the owner's laptop (NVIDIA GeForce RTX 3050 Laptop, 4 GB); the external agent over MCP is the Director route (B-9 closed as a decision); a draft pull request against `main` was asked for. `main` was created at the first commit and the draft PR opened (odedmoshe/PULSEFRAME#1).
- Speech recognition is built as an optional local engine (`apps/server/src/speech.ts`, worker `apps/server/speech/pf_speech.py`): setup state (`GET /api/v1/speech`, MCP `get_speech_status`), owner-only verified model download for the exact bytes shown, recognition as a durable cancelable job (`POST …/assets/{id}/transcribe`, MCP `transcribe_asset`), result checked and attached by the internal `attach_transcript` operation (one undoable transaction), button and download prompt in the Speech tab. Phrases where the recogniser fell into repetition are left out and counted; a file with no recognised speech attaches nothing.
- Engine and model: faster-whisper 1.2.1 (CTranslate2 4.9.0) from PyPI; `ivrit-ai/whisper-large-v3-turbo-ct2@72ad623a` (Apache-2.0 by its model card; 1,621,665,181 bytes in five pinned files). Downloaded once under the owner's grant: 1.62 GB of the 5 GB allowed. A further 0.49 GB (`Systran/faster-whisper-small`) was fetched into the scratch area as a one-off cross-check and is not part of the product.
- Verified here: `pnpm proof:speech` 19/19 (`docs/evidence/speech-proof.json`) on 12 real Hebrew read-speech recordings (FLEURS he_il dev, CC BY 4.0, fetched to `.artifacts`, not committed): 18.8% of reference words not recognised, 30.6% word error rate in order (inflated by recordings whose speaker read clauses in another order than the reference text; cross-checked on one clip); every word span contains sound; refusals, cancel, undo, overwrite, cut by recognised word ids. `speech.test.ts` (6). The editor button was driven once in a browser by hand-written script (30 words appeared); it is not yet part of a proof.
- First owner-supplied footage (five phone clips, 720×1280, 17.7–30 fps constant, 7–15 s): `pnpm check:media <folder>` 8/8 (`docs/evidence/real-media-check.json`, technical figures only): all imported without conform, lengths match, placed, recognised (four with text; one produced only repetition and attached nothing), captioned, exported 51.5 s H.264/AAC; mid-clip brightness and per-second sound level of each clip match the source at its place in the export.
- Not established: accuracy on conversational or noisy speech; word-timing accuracy (no reference timing exists here; SPEC Appendix C target unmeasured); forced alignment of a typed transcript; filler-word detection; English or mixed-language speech with this Hebrew-tuned model; GPU speed (CPU here: roughly 8–10 s of wall time per short clip, most of it loading the model for each job; single run). Nobody watched or listened to the export of the owner's clips.
- 471 unit tests.

**Checkpoint 17b — local narration (B-8)**
- Narration from text is built on the same optional local service: `POST …/narration`, MCP `generate_narration`, a form in the Sound tab; the result is a generated audio asset with its recipe in provenance (`provenance.voice`), optionally placed at a time. A reference voice is accepted only from the owner's session with a consent statement, which is stored with the asset.
- Engine and models: chatterbox-tts 0.1.7 (PyTorch 2.6.0) in its own environment; `ResembleAI/chatterbox@5bb1f6ee` multilingual files (3.21 GB, MIT by model card) and `Phonikud/phonikud-onnx@b806189f` (0.31 GB, MIT by model card). Downloaded under the owner's grant: 3.52 GB of the 5 GB allowed.
- Found by the proof and fixed: unpointed Hebrew came out garbled (a second model recovered 8 of 18 words). The engine's own Hebrew step fails silently with the published diacritics package, so the worker now points Hebrew itself before synthesis (17 of 18 words recovered). Also found: the engine cannot load without `pkg_resources`; the setup state now reports that instead of failing at the first job.
- Verified here: `pnpm proof:voice` 19/19 (`docs/evidence/voice-proof.json`); the Sound-tab form driven once in a browser by a hand-written script (a clip appeared); not yet part of a proof.
