Likeness of a reference voice, other languages, long scripts, numbers and foreign words, GPU speed: all unassessed. CPU here: about 40 s per sentence.

**Checkpoint 17c — what recognition and narration make possible**
- Filler candidates (`POST …/analysis/fillers`, MCP `find_filler_words`, "Filler words" in the Speech tab): rules over the transcript's words — hesitation sounds in Hebrew and English, a word said twice in a row, words the user lists — with the timeline cuts to remove them as one undoable edit. No model. On the 12 read recordings it finds nothing, as expected for read speech; recognisers often leave hesitations out, and the panel says so.
- Word timing for typed or imported transcripts (`transcribe` with `mode: "align"`, "Time the words from the sound" in the Speech tab): the text and phrase ids stay; each phrase's words take the times of the words heard inside it (edit-distance alignment, `packages/core/src/align.ts`). Words the recogniser has no counterpart for get estimated times and their phrase is marked approximate. Attached by the internal `attach_word_timing` operation. This is alignment through recognition, not a dedicated forced aligner.
- Script narration (`POST …/narration/script`, MCP `narrate_script`, the same Sound-tab box): text is split into sentences (`sentences.ts`), one job and one clip per sentence, placed one after another with a gap on one track; a canceled or failed sentence leaves no hole. Up to 60 sentences per request.
- Reference voice in the editor: a tick box in the narration form takes the voice from the selected clip's media (5 s or more), and the button stays disabled until the speaker is named and the consent statement is ticked. The same server rule as before applies (owner session only; statement stored with the asset).
- Verified here: `pnpm proof:speech` 26/26 (adds: filler search on recognised and typed transcripts with exact removal, alignment of an imported SRT phrase — 13 of 13 words identical to what was heard on that one clip — and the recognition button and filler search driven in the editor); `pnpm proof:voice` 23/23 (adds: script of three sentences with the middle one canceled, the 60-sentence limit, and the narration and reference-voice forms driven in the editor); unit tests for fillers, sentence splitting and alignment (17 new, two with generated cases).
- Not established: alignment quality beyond one clean read sentence; word-timing accuracy in general; filler detection on real hesitant speech (none was available: the owner's clips contain none that the recogniser wrote down); long scripts; how any narration sounds.

Single run on this CPU: recognition of a short clip went from about 9 s to about 6 s; a narration sentence from about 40 s to about 15 s after the first (37 s with the load). Not a performance figure.

**Checkpoint 18i — creative tools: speed curves, motions, sound library, gallery, caption looks, pacing; three waves of parallel workers**
- **Speed curves** (`packages/core/src/speedCurves.ts`, op `apply_speed_curve`, picker in the clip panel): 14 named curves — the eight the specification names (Montage, Hero, Bullet, Flash In, Flash Out, Jump Cut, Smooth Ramp, Stutter; R-A.7) and six more (slow down, build up, wave, emphasis, surge, fast forward) — plus a hand-made curve from 2–16 points drawn on a graph (mouse or keyboard). A curve replaces the clip by one ordinary clip per segment, each at one speed or with a gradual change; every part runs up to an exact share of the source and is rounded down to whole frames, so rounding never accumulates and the source is never read past the old end. Linked sound is divided the same way. Clips after it on the same tracks (and what is linked to them) follow by default; other tracks, markers and hand-timed captions do not move. The shapes and numbers are this project's own: they were written from descriptions of what such curves do, not matched against another editor.
- **Motion presets** (`motionPresets.ts`, op `apply_motion_preset`): 53 motions — 19 entrances, 13 exits, 13 loops, 8 whole-clip moves — written as keyframe recipes on x, y, scale, rotation and opacity, relative to the clip's resting transform. An entrance and an exit merge by range; a loop or a move replaces the keys of its properties. A fifth tab in the effects gallery shows them as thumbnails that animate under the pointer.
- **Effects gallery** (`EffectGallery.tsx`): effects, colour looks, transitions, text styles and motions as live thumbnails drawn from the frame on screen by the same evaluate + compositor as preview and export; search; one click applies through the registered op.
- **Sound effects**: the built-in set grew from 7 to **80** formula-synthesised sounds in ten categories (`apps/server/src/sfx.ts`; the seven original graphs are unchanged, asserted by test), with a browser in the Sound tab (search, category chips, listen before adding, add at the playhead) and a route that serves the same bytes for listening (`GET /api/v1/sfx/:id/audio`). Nobody has listened to the 73 new sounds: they are checked for length, level, determinism and silence after the end only.
- **Caption looks**: 40 ready-made caption looks (`captionPresets.ts`, op `apply_caption_look`, selector in the caption controls) built only from fields the caption renderer already has and the five bundled fonts; readable contrast is enforced by test, appearance on footage is not judged.
- **Pacing report** (`pacing.ts`, panel in the Deliver tab): cuts and words per five-second window as a heat strip, totals, and warnings (long static picture, rapid cuts, dense speech, fast captions, long gap in speech), each with a jump to the place. Measured from the document only (clip boundaries, word timing, caption cues); no picture or sound analysis.
- **Colour lookup tables**: a strict `.cube` parser with the helpers a shader pass needs (`lut.ts`, 17 tests). Not wired to import or to a GPU pass yet: nothing in the editor uses it. *(Wired since, on branch `wt/lutwire`: see "Colour tables wired" under the run instructions below.)*
- **ComfyUI**: a second model capability descriptor, `comfy.image.upscale-4x` (Real-ESRGAN x4plus through core nodes). Never executed; the model is a third-party mirror tagged BSD-3-Clause whose original publisher licence was not fetched, so it is recorded as `commercialUse: unknown`, `licenseReviewed: not_assessed`.
- **Verified here:** `pnpm proof:creative` 29/29 (new, `docs/evidence/creative-tools-proof.json`): a Hero curve on every exported frame (178 frames; the source never runs backwards, each part starts on the frame its `sourceIn` names and plays at the average speed it declares; picture and sound end together), all 14 curves through the API, undo; a motion on rendered stills, entrance and exit combined, all 53 through the API; in the editor: the curve tiles, the hand-made curve by keyboard, the gallery (40 effect thumbnails drawn on the software adapter, search, a look applied, Escape and focus), the motion tab (53 thumbnails, one applied), the sound browser (80 rows, filter, listen, stop, add), the pacing strip, the caption-look selector; no console errors. Unit tests for each library (speed curves incl. a property test over random points, motions, pacing, LUT, caption looks, sounds).
- **Not established:** how any curve, motion, look or sound looks or sounds to a person; any of it on the owner's machine; the gallery and motion thumbnails on a hardware adapter; the text-clip tabs of the gallery in a browser; the upscale capability in any way.
- A bug found by the proof and fixed: pressing "listen" twice before a sound had loaded started it again instead of cancelling.

**Checkpoint 18j — design refresh; sound never lands on top of sound by itself**
- Owner direction (2026-11-16): a marked upgrade in design. Done in one pass, by three workers and the lead:
  - the media library is a grid of thumbnail cards (filmstrip frame for video with hover scrub, the picture itself for images, the waveform for sound; length badge, in-use mark, three always-reachable actions; notes about a file fold away);
  - a consistent icon language (`lucide-react` 1.73.0, ISC; the notices check lists it) in the header, transport, edit bar, clip menu, track headers and inspector tabs, with every accessible name unchanged;
  - a start panel on an empty project (drop zone, file chooser, three steps, real shortcuts);
  - a visual layer (`apps/web/src/theme.css`, loaded last) for surfaces, buttons, fields, tabs, transport and timeline. Its generic rules carry no specificity (`:where`), so component geometry is untouched; the timeline area and side panels are larger.
- Sound placement: a clip placed without naming a track no longer lands on top of another clip on a sound track (two stacked sounds hid each other on the timeline); naming a track still allows the overlap crossfades need. A sound-only file added from the library starts at the playhead.
- The play button states playing/paused as an attribute; eight proofs that read the old glyph were updated. Phase 3 waits for the new title to be selected before pressing K (the revision number could update a moment earlier).
- **Verified here on the new design:** accessibility audit (no findings, Hebrew and English, all states); Phase 0 55, 3 53 (visual baselines rewritten for the new design), 4 20, 5 34, 6 37, 7 31, 8 21, 9 14, 11 12, 12 20, GPU 53, speed 19, browser export 23, editor interactions 25, owner UI 9, creative 29; 546 unit tests; notices check (11 packages).
- Two observations from this run: the owner-UI proof timed out once when run directly after other proofs and passed when rerun alone (explained and fixed in 18k: it counted the project list before it had loaded); two servers started by workers for smoke runs were found still listening and were stopped (they made Phase 5 fail to start on its port).
- **Not established:** the new design on the owner's machine and at other window sizes than 1440×900 and 1280×720; a screen reader; whether the look is what the owner wants (nobody but the lead has seen it).

**Checkpoint 18k — effects that follow the music; layouts; AI tools panel; folding sections**
- **Effects that follow the music** (an original feature; the surveyed editors trigger on beats, they do not bind an effect's phase to the detected beat grid): `beatPositionAt` (`packages/core/src/beatgrid.ts`) gives where a timeline moment falls in the beats of the music playing there — through the music clip's place, source start and speed, uneven beats and bars — and the evaluator puts it on the frame graph. Four effects read it when their new `follow` parameter is set to `music`: the zoom pulse, the strobe, the camera shake (a jolt on each hit) and the RGB shake; `every` hits on every Nth beat counted from the bar. The music is the first unmuted sound clip with a beat analysis playing at that moment. With no such music the effect keeps its own steady tempo and the panel says so. *(Superseded in 18m: the rest of this sentence described a per-strobe limit that did not hold for several strobes together, and its thinning statement over-claimed; see 18m for the timeline-wide schedule that replaced it.)* One strobe on one clip never starts more than three flashes in any one second, where a flash start is every moment its strength rises through half of its amount, the first frame of the clip included. The reason: the strength is highest at a flash time and only falls until the next one, and two flash times are at least 0.345 s apart (`STROBE_MIN_GAP` in `gpu.ts`: a third of a second plus a third of the 35 ms a flash stays above half strength, which pays for a flash that is first seen late: on the first frame of a clip that begins while it is still bright, or on a frame just after it). Steady: the flash times are k / rate on the timeline; a rate above 2.93 flashes 2.93 times a second (`STROBE_MAX_RATE`, the same sum for the steady flash; the schema still accepts up to 3), and `rate`, `amount` and `every` cannot be keyframed (`NOT_ANIMATABLE` in `effects.ts`; keys a document already holds are not used when drawing). Following the music: the flash times are followed beats whose number, counted from the first bar start and no longer from each bar (this now holds for all four effects), is a multiple of `every`; fast music is thinned to every second, third… of them; a beat less than 0.345 s after another kept beat does not flash. The beats of all the pieces of the music are judged together as they are heard (a cut clip, a speed change in steps, a speed curve, other music followed just before), so a cut in the music no longer silences the third of a second after it. Where no analysed music plays the strobe does not flash at all (for the strobe this replaces the fallback to its steady rate described in the sentence before; the panel's note, the catalog text and the row in `CAPABILITIES.md` still describe the fallback). Established by `packages/engine/src/strobe.test.ts`: property tests (fast-check) over random beat lists with gaps of 0.05 to 2 s, with and without bars, any `every`, speeds, gradual speed changes, trims, splits, music cut into pieces or replaced by a stepped speed change or a speed curve, a second piece of music, and over any steady rate; each case at one of seven frame rates (24, 25, 30, 60, 23.976, 29.97, 59.94), with the title cut into up to four pieces that each keep the strobe, sampled 240 times a second and once per rendered frame through `evaluate` and the strobe's own parameter function, counting flash starts in every period of one second from the first sample on and refusing any other rise; plus the reported cases (bars with thinning, a trimmed or split gradual speed change, a keyframed rate, a clip that begins just after a flash time, music in pieces). `musicHit.test.ts` and `beatgrid.test.ts` cover the rules and the beat places. **Not established, and known not to hold:** several strobes with different settings at once or right after one another (two on one clip; clips that overlap or follow one another with different steady rates, or one steady and one following the music; a clip and the next one during a transition). Each keeps to the limit, what they add up to is not limited: six flash starts in one second were measured for steady rates 3 and 2.5 on one title, four for a title cut in two with rates 3 and 2.9. The strobe's parameter function sees only its own effect; a limit over all of them needs the flash times of every strobe of the sequence at the frame, in the evaluator. Also open: a document that already holds keyframes on a strobe's `rate`, `amount` or `every` is reported as invalid, and every further edit of it is refused until those keys, the strobe or its clip are removed (no migration exists); a first frame that shows less than half of a flash that fell before the clip began is not counted as a flash start; pixels (these are the numbers the shader receives, not a rendered frame).
- **Layouts** (`layouts.ts`, op `apply_layout`, picker shown when two or more picture clips are selected): 17 layouts — side by side, top and bottom, columns, rows, 2×2 and 3×3 grids, a main picture with smaller ones, two-thirds splits, picture-in-picture in each corner, a centred window. Each clip gets a position, a size and, where it is cropped to its cell, a rectangle mask. Cells are filled from the bottom track up; the op does not move clips in time or between tracks.
- **AI tools panel** (`AiTools.tsx`, in the library): the published ComfyUI capabilities as cards with their real state from the server (runnable, or exactly why not: model missing and its size, licence not confirmed, models directory not set), a picture selector and a run button, and plain steps for connecting ComfyUI when it is not connected. The existing diagnostic button is kept.
- **Folding sections** in the clip tab (speed, position and size, entrance and exit, look and effects, analysis, text), all open by default, remembered per browser, with open-all / close-all.
- **Verified here:** `pnpm proof:gpu` 55/55 — two new checks: on a clip under a generated 94-bpm drum loop whose beats were found by the product's own beat analysis, the stills at the frame nearest a beat and at the frame latest in a beat match the pulse computed from the detected beats within 3 pixels, and with the music track muted the effect falls back to its tempo setting. `pnpm proof:creative` 30/30 — new: a layout for the wrong number of pictures is refused; with two picture clips selected the picker offers the two-picture layouts and "side by side" puts the lower track's picture in the left half and the upper one in the right half, each cropped to its cell, on a rendered still. Unit tests: `beatgrid.test.ts`, `musicHit.test.ts`, `layouts.test.ts` (geometry by formula for three frame shapes, four picture shapes, both fit settings, and a property test).
- Regression on this state: 558 unit tests; accessibility audit (no findings); Phase 0 55, 3 53 (baselines rewritten for the sections), 6 37, 7 31, 8 21, 12 20, editor interactions 25, owner UI 9 (four runs in a row), GPU 55, creative 30. Three proofs had races that the heavier panel exposed and were made to wait for the state they depend on instead of reading it in the same instant: Phase 12 typed a new caption's text before the new caption was selected; the creative proof read the focus hand-back before the next tick; the owner-UI proof counted project rows before the list had loaded (this was the intermittent failure noted in 18j, not the loop-playback wait). None was a fault of the editor.
- **Not established:** the AI tools panel in its ready state against a real ComfyUI (a worker exercised it against a stub that answers readiness only; no job was run through it); how the following effects feel against real music (the drum loop is synthetic); layouts on real footage; the strobe thinning on a rendered frame (unit-tested only); the layout geometry against an independent implementation (the renderer exports none; the still check covers one layout).

**Checkpoint 18l — text animation by letter, word or line; graphic elements; timeline zoom control**
- **Text animation** (`TextAnimationSchema`, op `set_text_animation`, controls in the title panel): the letters, words or lines of a title come in one after another (fade, typewriter, rise, drop, pop, slide, zoom), go out (fade, sink, lift, shrink, slide) and can move while shown (wave, bounce, pulse, jitter) — 16 animations over three units. Units follow reading order, so Hebrew starts from the right; a letter keeps its vowel points (grapheme clusters); a word that mixes Hebrew with digits or Latin is placed run by run. The pose of each unit is plain arithmetic (`packages/engine/src/textAnimation.ts`) that the compositor only applies; outlines are drawn before fills so a thick outline never covers a neighbour. When nothing is animating the title goes through the ordinary drawing path, so it is identical to a title without animation. This is separate from keyframes and motions, which move the whole title.
- **Graphic elements** (`packages/core/src/elements.ts`, `apps/server/src/elements.ts`, route `POST /projects/:id/elements`, agent tool `add_graphic_element`, browser in the library): **252** original elements in eight categories (arrows 45, shapes 44, badges 23, bubbles 25, frames 20, title bars 21, lines 26, symbols 48), each an SVG produced by formula in this build from a closed set of drawing elements (no text, images, scripts or external references; enforced by test) and rasterised to a transparent PNG by ffmpeg's SVG decoder. An added element is an ordinary picture asset and clip. If the machine's ffmpeg cannot read SVG the route answers 501 and says so; it does not substitute anything. A worker looked at all eight contact sheets and the lead at two; brush strokes, wobble arrows and S-curve arrows are the weakest.
- **Timeline zoom**: a visible control (out, logarithmic slider, in, fit) and Ctrl/⌘+wheel zoom around the pointer; zooming keeps the playhead, or the centre of the view, in place.
- **Verified here:** `pnpm proof:creative` 38/38 — new: typewriter by letter on an English title (one letter on the left at the first frame, more ink at every sample, byte-identical to the plain title from the end of the entrance on); fade out by word; a wave loop (repeatable, differs between frames, same amount of ink); the same on a Hebrew title, which starts at the right end; an entrance plus exit longer than the title refused; the title panel and the zoom buttons in the editor; graphic elements through the API (PNG with alpha, provenance, three-second clip, the asked colour on a rendered still, refusals) and through the browser in the library. Unit tests: `textAnimation.test.ts`, `elements.test.ts` (core: 252 elements against the markup rules; server: rasterised samples, alpha, determinism), `zoom.math.test.ts`.
- Regression on this state: 598 unit tests; accessibility audit (no findings); Phase 0 55, 3 53 (baselines rewritten), 4 20, 6 37, 7 31, 8 21, 12 20, GPU 55, speed 19, editor interactions 25, owner UI 9, creative 38. Afterwards, on the same commit, the long suites were rerun and passed: render parity (pass; small-text warnings as before), seek (7 configurations, 0 wrong frames), long media 6/6, start offsets 4/4, images 3/3 (22 shown correctly, 2 refused as intended), project settings 2/2 (15), render fuzz 2/2 (10 sessions), inputs 4/4 (30 exported correctly, 1 with a noted difference; the earlier run recorded 28 and 2, and which shape changed class was not looked into), Phase 5 34, 9 14, 11 12, browser export 23, pitch 12, reverse sound 7, soak 7. The first attempt at this run was cut off by a restart of the session's worker during the long-media suite; it was started again from that suite. `proof:speech` and `proof:voice` were not rerun (nothing they cover changed).
- **Not established:** how the text animations look in motion (stills only; nobody watched them play); mixed-direction titles by letter on a rendered frame (unit placement is by construction, the proof covers pure English and pure Hebrew); whether the ffmpeg on the owner's Windows machine can read SVG (if not, elements will report that); trackpad pinch on a real device (wheel events were synthesised); the colours and size of an element are not recorded in its provenance (the asset schema allows id, version and generator only).

**Checkpoint 18m — independent review and its fixes; colour tables; one flash schedule for all strobes; ids that name Object.prototype members**
- **Independent review of 18i–18l.** 36 agents in six areas (core ops, the beat grid and music-following effects, server routes, web panels, the later additions, media handling), each finding then checked by separate skeptics. **29 defects were confirmed**, among them three about the strobe's safety limit, and several of the lead's own claims were found to over-claim: the docs said 40 caption looks where the code has 39; the layout check in the creative proof passed without the masks it said it checked; the zoom "fit" check could not fail; the strobe thinning statement in 18k did not hold. All 29 were fixed in four worktrees (core, beat grid, server, web) plus one for media, each fix with a test that failed before it, then reviewed and repaired once more. Examples: `apply_speed_curve` keeps link groups intact and refuses a crossfaded clip with the reason; `placeTrack` never auto-places on an occupied track; text animations are refitted on split, cut, gap and trim; `apply_motion_preset` records what it replaced; a sound browser second press while loading no longer restarts playback; theme rules use `:where()` so they never override the geometry of canvas handles.
- **Colour tables** (formerly on branch `wt/lutwire`): a `.cube` file uploaded to the library becomes an asset of kind `lut` (parsed by `parseCube` at ingest, refused with a typed error otherwise); the effect `color.lut` (41st effect, WebGPU pass with the table as a second texture) applies it with an intensity; `apply_lut` sets it in one step; the Look panel has a chooser and an "import .cube" button; the library card shows the size and has no timeline actions; `remove_asset`, validation, undo, archive and export planning know the reference. **Verified here** (software adapter): `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm test` 630, `pnpm build`, `pnpm proof:gpu` 76/76 (21 colour-table checks: the still against core's `sampleStrip` within 3 of 255 on 288 pixels, measured 0.5; intensity 0, 0.5, 1; identity table; domains 0..2, 0..1.5 and 0.0625..0.9375 against the file's own definition, measured 0.9; translucent pixels and the top of every channel on a second fixture, measured 0.8; preview = still; exported frame; refusals; the editor's notice while a table cannot be read and its recovery by itself), `pnpm proof:phase7` 31/31, `pnpm proof:creative` 38/38. **After review** (same branch): export planning counts the table and file of a clip that is only drawn as the outgoing picture of a transition; a refused `.cube` no longer carries file text back in its error; a `.cube` name longer than 256 characters is still read as a table; a table in a project archive must parse and fit the size limit like an upload; a table id such as `__proto__` is `not_found`, not a crash; a non-unit input domain is resampled on a finer grid when the file's own would lose accuracy; the media pool does not fetch or parse a failing table for every drawn frame and picks up a repaired file; the editor says over the preview when the frame is shown without its table. **Not verified:** a hardware adapter, real footage, third-party LUT files, the other proofs and conformance suites (not rerun on this branch), anyone looking at the pictures. **Known limits:** a table whose input domain fits no grid of at most 65 entries per axis is an approximation (up to 4.5 of 255 measured in the steepest cell of a gamma-type table; exact would need the domain in the shader); the clamp of the straight colour to 0..1 in the pass is not reachable from a valid premultiplied picture and is therefore not proven by pixels; the older op `rename_asset` writes to `Object.prototype` when given the id `__proto__` (found in review, not fixed on this branch).
- **One flash schedule for all strobes (safety).** The review showed the three-flashes-a-second rule held for one strobe but not for two (two steady strobes at 3 and 2.5 a second on one title started six flashes in a second). Now no strobe decides when it flashes: `packages/engine/src/strobeSchedule.ts` lists, once per document version and sequence, every flash any strobe would like to start (steady: k / rate; following the music: its thinned beats from `followedBeats` in `beatgrid.ts`), moves each to the first frame that shows it, and keeps a flash only if the last kept flash of ANY strobe started at least `flashGapFrames(fps)` = ⌈fps/3⌉ + 1 frames earlier. The evaluator gives each strobe the seconds since its last kept start; the strength is amount·e^(−20·s), exactly zero after 0.6 s. Pieces of a cut clip that lie back to back and carry the same strobe flash as one run. A clip never shows the rest of a flash from before its start. Consequences, now said in the UI texts (being changed in a worker branch): with several strobes or fast music some flashes are left out; a strobe that follows the music shows nothing where no analysed music plays. Older documents that carry keys on strobe parameters that may no longer be animated stay valid and editable (the keys are inert; no op adds new ones). Flashing built by other means (keyframed opacity, cuts between bright and dark pictures, the flash transition, other effects) is **not** limited by this schedule; a skeptic is measuring the other built-ins now.
- **Ids that name Object.prototype members.** `rename_asset` with the id `__proto__` wrote a `name` onto `Object.prototype` (found in the LUT review). Every op and recipe step now refuses an id-like argument (`id`, `…Id`, `…Ids`, an effect's `lut`) equal to such a name with `not_found` before the op runs; ops that already check an id with `Object.hasOwn` and give a better error (the colour-table ops) declare it and keep their message. Text fields keep such words. The server still has direct lookups of the same kind on request ids (e.g. asset routes); they read only, and are on the list for the server pass.
- **Proofs made sensitive.** `pnpm proof:creative`: the side-by-side layout is also rendered with the masks taken away and the seam pixels must change (measured: blue without masks, red with them); "fade out by word" must show the first and last word with different shares of ink left during the exit (measured 0.10 vs 1.00); the zoom check zooms out as well and requires "fit" to shrink a far-zoomed view to fill at least half of it (2,049 px → 1,058 of 1,500). `pnpm proof:gpu` 77: new, two strobes on a picture and a strobing title over it, exported through the GPU worker: flash starts every 11 frames, at most 3 in any 30-frame window, 2.8 a second.
- **Verified here** (full regression on `6ad00bd`, then the two changed proofs on `7e0685c`): typecheck; 823 unit tests (826 after the id guard); build; notices; Phase 0 55/55, 3 53/53 (baselines rewritten), 4 20, 5 34, 6 37, 7 31, 8 21, 9 14, 11 12, 12 20; GPU 76/76 then 77/77; speed 19; browser export 23; editor interactions 25; owner UI 9; creative 38/38 (before and after strengthening); pitch 12; reverse sound 7; accessibility audit (no findings in either language); soak 7; conformance: start offsets 4/4, inputs 4/4 (30 correct, 1 with a noted difference), images 3/3, project settings 2/2, render fuzz 2/2, parity pass (30/30 exact frames with and without video), seek 7 configurations × 1,000 random + 300 sequential, 0 wrong.
- **Not established:** a following strobe on a rendered frame with real music; how any of it looks or feels to a person; the LUT on a hardware adapter or with third-party `.cube` files.

**Checkpoint 18n — flash safety made whole (ADR-0009); the review follow-ups merged; the next wave designed**
- **Four skeptics attacked the flash schedule** (an independent sampler over random documents built only from ops; every path by which a strobe reaches pixels; every other built-in that flashes, measured with the guideline's definitions; arithmetic and limits), each finding then reproduced by a separate verifier. What they found and what was done:
  - *The preview in real time* (high): shuttle at 2×–8× showed 6–12 flash starts a second; loop wraps and edits during playback were never checked against what was on screen. **Fixed:** the editor's player passes every frame through `FlashGuard` (engine), which follows the strobe light it lets through and holds back any rise that would begin less than a third of a second after the last one, whatever the transport does. Normal-speed playback is unchanged. `flashGuard.test.ts`: shuttle ±2/4/8× (fails without the guard), paint jitter, loop, edits mid-play, rocking the playhead across one flash, and a property test over random transports (300 runs).
  - *Half-drawn frames on screen* (found while proving the above in the browser): `drawGraph` paints the background and then waits mid-frame for pictures and GPU passes; drawn on the visible canvas, the screen could show those states (the 8× sampler measured 16 brightness swings in a second). **Fixed:** the player draws off-screen and copies the finished frame in one step.
  - *Cut pieces with different settings* (high): a later piece with a higher amount (or another colour) showed the age of the earlier piece's flash at its own brightness, so the light rose at the cut without a scheduled start (6 a second). **Fixed:** pieces join a run only with identical settings. Flash frames are stored as Float64 (an Int32Array wrapped at absurd frame rates the schema accepts).
  - *Other built-ins* (high/medium): flash and dip transitions on short back-to-back clips, the flicker motion preset at its defaults on 50/60 fps projects, the old-film flicker at full amount, fast wave, glitch and shake, a music-following pulse on fast beats, the beat montage on fast music. **Decided (ADR-0009):** only the strobe is hard-limited; built-ins are made safe at their defaults (done: film flicker swing under a tenth of the luminance range for every seed; the wave continues the picture's edge instead of uncovering dark strips; motion presets keep their seconds at any frame rate and opacity loops refuse periods that would dim more than three times a second; the beat montage leaves out cuts closer than a third of a second and says so); everything else is checked on the rendered result by `FlashAnalyser` (engine: 24 × 24 cells, transitions with hysteresis, more than a quarter of a 10-degree window flashing more than three times in a second fails; red flashes likewise; tested at 24/30/60 fps, area, red, ranges and a property over square waves). Wiring the check into export, a job and the Deliver tab is **not built yet**.
  - Low items recorded, not changed: a strobe at the top rate can leave another with few flashes (the strobe texts say some flashes are left out); a flash wanted inside the last frame of a cut piece is dropped (safe direction); without WebGPU the preview drops the strobe without a notice over the picture; the gallery's strobe tile is drawn after the flash has faded; a GIF of a 60 fps project gets 1-centisecond frame delays.
- **Ids that name Object.prototype members** are refused by every op (above, 18m).
- **Review follow-ups merged** (three worktrees, each fix with a test that failed before it): core — no edit leaves a clip alone in a link group (`dissolveLoneLinks`; lone ids in older projects stay valid, `loneLinkClips` lists them); `animate_clip` and the motion library place entrances and exits through one function, whichever runs last owns its end; the 40th caption look "Karaoke on paper"; truthful strobe wording. Web — one edit at a time on the ramp buttons; speed-curve tiles held with a reason for clips the op refuses; canvas handles clipped to the viewer with stand-ins at its edge; one shared thumbnail/waveform cache per file; the AI tools panel shows only its own job and an honest error with a retry; gallery transitions ask for a length the clips allow; strobe texts. Server — one storage ceiling for every surface (the agent's export tool, built-in sounds and narration were not held to it); element picture version 2; a failed archive copy no longer leaves its temporary file. The reviewers of these branches are still running; their repairs will be merged on top.
- **Verified here** after the merges: `pnpm test` 906; build; `pnpm proof` 55/55; Phase 3 53/53 (run three times: once one check failed on two 404s for an asset's thumbnails requested before they existed, an intermittent race to look at; the other runs passed), 4 20/20, 5 34/34, 7 31/31, 8 21/21; GPU 78/78 (new: the 8× shuttle in the editor sampled on every animation frame — 7 flashes in 2.5 s, at most 3 in any second, 11 frames held back); browser export 23/23; editor interactions 25/25; owner UI 9/9; creative 38/38; speed 19/19; accessibility audit (no findings).
- **Designed (read-only workflows, two designs and a judge per track):** wave 1 of sound effects (nine DSP effects — telephone, megaphone, radio, robot, 8-bit, echo, reverb in four rooms, bass boost, stereo width — and eleven presets, one TypeScript implementation shared by preview, server export and browser export, exact seek and warm-up rules; ADR-0010 to come) and a visual wave (echo trails, time-split colour, shutter motion blur, stutter; snow, confetti, hearts, bokeh, speed lines; a date stamp; old-digicam and CRT looks; whip and spin transitions; beat modulation of geometry and texture parameters). The shared contracts of the sound effects are in the tree (`audioEffects.ts`, the `audioFx` field and its validation, the engine's kernel/registry/voice interfaces, the plan types, `voicefx-fixtures.ts`, tested); three workers are building it now, and one is writing the visual wave's contracts.
- **Not established:** any of the new or changed effects judged by eye on real footage; the screen guard on a real display (headless Chromium sampled on animation frames); the analyser against a certified analyser.

**Checkpoint 18o — sound effects and a visual wave, built by six workers and merged; a decoder race found and fixed**
- **Sound effects, wave 1 (ADR-0010).** Nine effects (telephone, megaphone, radio, robot, 8-bit, echo, reverb in four rooms, bass boost, stereo width) and eleven presets (incl. stadium announcer and inner thoughts) on audio clips, with five ops, the «אפקטי קול» block in the Sound tab, a timeline mark and tail, and agent views (`list_catalog`, timeline, `describe_moment` with tails). One TypeScript DSP implementation is used by the preview, the server export and the browser export; ranges render exactly as the full render (bit-exact within the warm-up, within 2/32768 beyond); echo and reverb ring past the clip and are cut with a short fade at the end of the video. The browser export now plans at its output rate (a latent bug for projects not at 48 kHz). Reviews found and the workers fixed: an export range starting in a tail left the decoded source out of the manifest; the browser export dropped extra channels instead of downmixing; two quick auditions in a row could leave one that could not be stopped; the UI proof crashed before half its checks; a slider announced 4 repeats as "400%".
- **Visual wave 1 (ADR-0011).** Time effects (echo trails, time-split colour, shutter motion blur, stutter on a steady rate or the music's beats) drawn by reading the clip at exact earlier times through extra decode lanes, merged in exact integers; overlays (snow, confetti, hearts, bokeh, speed lines) and a seven-segment date stamp, seeded and in closed form per frame; old-digicam and CRT looks and whip and spin transitions as GPU passes; a "beat" toggle that makes a geometry or texture parameter kick on the music's beats (brightness and colour cannot). Decoders are counted per frame (editor 10, stills and exports 12); a frame over the budget is refused with its reason, and preflight lists the frames an export would refuse. Reviews found and the workers fixed: snow at its extremes flashed (a speed limit now keeps every setting under the photosensitivity threshold; hearts and confetti too), and animated density or size made particles jump; a GPU pass whose shader the adapter rejects silently reused the previous pass's pixels (now unavailable with the adapter's message); preflight over-counted decoders; the editor's notice for a frame over the budget said the material failed to load (now its own sentence, no retries, Hebrew wording).
- **A decoder race, found by the regression (two causes).** "VideoSample is closed" appeared intermittently in the editor page (Phase 6 2 of 3 before the wave, Phase 3 1 of 3 after). (1) Pinning used one shared counter, so a drawing that began (the source viewer's player shares the editor's pool) released the decoders of a drawing still in progress; now each drawing pins with its own token until it ends. (2) When a preview proxy became ready, or a clip switched to another file, the decoder was closed at once even while a drawing was about to paint from it; it is now closed when that drawing ends. A unit test reproduces each cause (fails with the old code). With both fixes: Phase 3 53/53 five runs in a row, Phase 6 37/37.
- **Proof faults fixed (the checks, not the product), each with its measurement:** sound C compared a lossy Opus file in bands 30–45 dB down, at the codec's noise floor (the browser's mix before encoding is within 0.15 dB of the server in every band); D2's points after the sweep were below the measurement's rounding; D3's floor of 20 tail windows was miscalculated (16 expected; 17 measured, within 0.27 dB); the overlays proof used `1 << 31` (negative in JavaScript) and joined lavfi options with `=`; the time proof judged "differs from the plain title" by a whole-frame mean; the creative proof sampled a slowly drawn tile for a fixed 2.2 s.
- **Verified here** (full regression on `30e20de` plus the fixes after it): typecheck; 1252 unit tests (1255 after the decoder tests); build; notices; Phase 0 55/55, 3 53/53 (one run 52/53, the race above), 4 20/20, 5 34/34, 6 37/37, 7 32/32, 8 21/21, 9 14/14, 11 12/12, 12 20/20; GPU 90/90; speed 19/19; browser export 23/23; editor interactions 29/29; owner UI 9/9; creative 41/41; pitch 12/12; reverse sound 7/7; time effects 26/26; overlays 40/40; sound effects 29/29, preview 9/9, UI and agents 26/26; accessibility audit (no findings); soak 7/7; conformance: start offsets 4/4, inputs 4/4 (30 correct, 1 with a noted difference), images 3/3, project settings 2/2, render fuzz 2/2, parity pass, seek pass.
- **Not established:** nobody has listened to the sound effects or looked at the new pictures on real footage; the new GPU passes (digicam, CRT, whip, spin) only on the software adapter; Node vs Chromium bit equality of the DSP (measured within tolerance only); the preview DSP's main-thread cost on a laptop (the check is load-sensitive here); the effect gallery draws about one frame a second in the headless software-adapter browser while a tile animates; keyframes on an overlay's speed or wind still move its particle field; a loud mix through bass boost and reverb can go about 1.5 dB over full scale, and the export then clips.

**Checkpoint 19a — wave 3 merged (trend pack, voice cleanup, colour match, motion tracking and privacy regions, templates and adjustment layers); the AI-model wave (ADR-0017); the flash check on both export routes; the foundation of the UI redesign; waves 4 and 5 designed**
- **How it was built.** Every track: shared contracts first (one implementer, an adversarial reviewer, a repair), then three workers in their own worktrees, each reviewed and repaired, then the lead merged, resolved the conflicts and ran the proofs on the merged tree. The decisions are in the ADRs: 0012 trend pack, 0013 voice cleanup, 0014 colour match, 0015 motion tracking (amended: tracker `pf-track 2`), 0016 adjustment layers and templates (schema version 2, identity migration), 0017 the AI-model wave with an amendment to ADR-0007 (a second provider `local`, frame jobs, one compute lane shared by export, speech and model runs).
- **What the merged tree now has** (details and limits in the ADRs): 18 more transitions (canvas and WebGPU; film-burn and light-leak lights join the flash schedule), transition options, text look fields and effects; hum removal, noise reduction and a speech enhancer as deterministic sound effects; colour match to a reference clip stored as ordinary grade parameters; model-free motion tracks per source frame, text and stickers that follow, privacy regions (blur, pixelate, cover) resolved per bare picture and applied to every copy an effect draws; adjustment tracks whose look applies to everything under them; templates with replace-media slots; photo upscale, smooth slow motion, video enhance, AI voice cleanup, caption translation Hebrew ↔ English (review "needed" until the owner marks it; agents cannot), classic vocal removal, portrait blur for stills — every model run a durable job, validated before success, entering the project only through ops. Model downloads are owner-only.
- **Faults the lead found while merging, and fixed** (each with a test that failed before): a privacy gap across two tracks — a clip with both a velocity-ramp transition and a privacy region resolved the region for the wrong source frame during the transition (`withPictureOf` in `packages/engine/src/evaluate.ts` now carries the regions of the picture it shows; `transitionOptions.test.ts`); the tracker latched onto a look-alike object (re-acquire must now match the seed, NCC ≥ 0.7, plus a motion gate); an adjustment clip's opacity fade was off by two levels (now faded with `destination-in`, then drawn); a transition could be put on an adjustment clip (refused); the operations fuzz never reached three ops after the generators were merged (generators aimed at valid targets; a focused generator added).
- **Checks that were wrong and were corrected, said plainly** (the product was right; each correction is stricter or equally strict and has a control): templates proof counted template elements as covering pictures; trend-transitions T2 (page-turn back shading) and T7 (compared against a 4:4:4 reference where the export is 4:2:0; now the same conversion plus a wrong-frame control); AI proofs assumed bit-exact transport where ComfyUI truncates in float32 (now checked against that arithmetic) and bit-identical batches (now ≤ 2 per byte, ≤ 1% differing, on PNG frames); tracking P4 (restricted to the frames where the decoy exists, with a control) and P11 (the barcode strip that legitimately flashes is painted grey, with a control and the measured peak) — these two were changed only after the owner approved them, because the permission system had refused the repair agent's edit and the lead did not work around it.
- **Flash check on both export routes (ADR-0009 step 4).** The browser export feeds every frame to `FlashAnalyser` and posts the verdict with the frame count and ranges; the server validates it (frames match, verdict consistent with ranges; otherwise 422 `invalid_photosensitivity`) and stores it marked `measuredBy: 'browser'`.
- **Verified here on the merged tree** (CPU profile, software WebGPU adapter): typecheck; 2157 unit tests in 191 files; build; trend-baseline (348 earlier frame hashes identical); trend-light 15/15; trend-transitions 15/15; Phase 7 33/33; sound effects 29/29 + 9/9 + 36/36; tracking 26/26; adjust 11/11; templates 70/70; AI local tools 20/20; AI frame jobs 16/16; AI trials 13/13; AI panel 15/15; G0 39/39; BiRefNet re-run 9/9; Phase 11 12/12; browser export 26/26.
- **Failing on the merged tree at this checkpoint (evidence committed as failing; repairs running, each followed by an adversarial verifier that checks no check was loosened):** voice cleanup 9/11 (C4: a constant −3-sample lag; C5: 0.61 dB where the bound is 0.5); trend-text 20/23 (X3 gradient bottom stop off by about 3.9; X3 long shadow one pixel short; X6 extrude-retro export PSNR 35.93); GPU 91/92 (the 8× shuttle check: the guard held back 0 frames; may be load).
- **Repairs landed (19b):** voice cleanup 11/11, trend-text 25/25 (trend baseline still 348 identical hashes), colour match 17/17, life line 14/14 (new; two checks corrected: the export comparison now uses the still as 4:2:0 can hold it with two controls, and the browser calendar check reads digits when this Chromium's Intl writes no Hebrew letters). Each repair was attacked by an independent verifier; for colour match the verifier found a false measurement in the repair's own explanation and a fit that minimised a different measure than the one it reports — both fixed in a second round (fit now finishes on its reported distance; two untested stages removed; panel tests that fail under their mutations). Still open: the GPU 8× shuttle check — the verifier showed the repaired check could pass a guard with a wrong gap under load; its follow-up waits for a quiet machine.

The wave 6 notes are now seen in a browser (the transcript panel's untimed-copy note; the archive import's toast, which has no timer and is not repeated after a reload; «החזרת החלקים שנמחקו» in the Templates bar, the recipes panel and the film bar), with an `audit:a11y` state for each of the three in both languages and themes. **Defect found:** in a new project (ripple delete on) Delete on a template picture shifts the later pictures, which then count as edited by hand, and both Shuffle and «החזרת החלקים שנמחקו» are refused with `template_blocked` (Shift+Delete or undo works; the film bar and the beat montage are not affected). Lead decision (provisional, in OWNER_REVIEW): a clip whose only change is its position in time is not "edited by hand"; regeneration puts it back on its slot and says so; trimmed or otherwise edited clips stay protected. - **Template defect fixed (2026-11-25), merged, verified-here (one run each):** template regeneration no longer treats a clip that was only moved in time as edited by hand (`movedOnly` in `packages/core/src/templateOps.ts`: the run now records where it placed each clip, `run.ranges[id] = {start, end, trackId}`, optional and additive, and the clip's fingerprint at its recorded start must equal the run's). After a Delete that closes the gap, Shuffle and «החזרת החלקים שנמחקו» work, the bar says how many items returned to their slots (notice `moved_back`, `movedBack` on the answer), and undo is exact (two steps after Delete and the button, three with a Shuffle between). Trimmed or otherwise edited clips stay protected; the `template_blocked` hint no longer proposes the request just refused (ADR-0016 amendment). `proof:templates` 147/147, `proof:editor-interactions` 79/79 (the either-outcome check replaced by two that require success), seeded regeneration property 40 runs (26 ok, 10 with items moved back, 12 blocked), undo property with ripple deletes 60 runs and 0 mismatches. Then merged (two more rounds, one run each on the worker's tree): (1) runs made before places were recorded are covered by working the places out from a plan made with the run's own stored parameters and verifying by fingerprint (`derivedRanges`): pictures of a template, a picture's own sound and pictures of a film are tested; titles, looks, sound effects and a template's music of an older run stay kept when moved (never a refusal); when the plan cannot be made as it was (template version changed) the refusal stays and its hint says why (`details.placesUnknown`). (2) For runs that record the track, a clip taken to another track is edited by hand and kept. (3) Family film and beat montage: lead decision corrected after the first build showed that a pick dragged by hand would no longer be kept — there the rule is narrow (`closedGapOnly`): a clip is laid out again only when it moved earlier by exactly the summed length of the run's own clips that are gone and lay before it on the same track (or moved exactly with a linked clip that did); a clip dragged by hand stays kept and the rest is laid around it, as before (ADR-0026 amendment; ADR-0016 says why templates keep the broad rule). The film bar says «קליפים שזזו בגלל מחיקה לא נשארו במקום שאליו זזו: הסרט סודר מחדש יחד איתם», the montage panel «חזרו לפעימות אחרי שזזו בגלל מחיקה: N»; the montage's `removed` names only places in fact left empty. `pnpm test` 5263 passed and 0 failed, `proof:templates` 147/147, `proof:family-film` 45/45 (F13 R1 and R3 in their original form, R4 the ripple case, F21 the button after Delete), `proof:phase11` 16/16 (a dragged clip stays, undo exact); seeded properties with drags beside ripple deletes, 40 runs each (film: 9 laid out again, 6 dragged kept, 0 refused; montage: 17 and 3). Recorded: Shift+Delete followed by a plain Delete before the same clips keeps them (the sum counts both deletes); older beat-montage runs keep moved clips.

- **Language-assistant privacy and cancel (2026-11-25), merged, verified-here (one run each, MOCK adapter only, no real provider):** confirmed by a test that failed 23 of 27 before the fix: any credential that may observe a project could read an assistant job's explanation, proposal id, cost and both error texts through `GET /jobs/:id`, MCP `get_job` and the project event stream, and the assistant's proposed operations through the proposal routes, `list_proposals` and the `proposal` event. Closed with one module, `apps/server/src/assistant/visibility.ts`: every principal other than the owner's session reads of an assistant job only id, project, type, actor, status and times; the assistant's proposals are not listed, answer 404 by id like an unknown id, are not rendered and are not announced to them (`assistant/jobPrivacy.test.ts`: planted words in the typed text, the explanation, the operations and two error texts; seven kinds of credential over every GET route, the job/proposal/assistant write routes, the read tools, resources, prompts and the live event stream; other write routes are not walked). A job row never stores the typed text (hash and length only), but it does store the explanation and an error sentence, so they are in the database and in backups; an archive holds no job and no proposal. The pending assistant row has «ביטול הבקשה»; a cancelled request says whether anything had been sent and may have been charged (`proof:command-bar` 23 + 6 contract-tested, check 11f). Markers on a clip showing a time-preserving derived copy are seen in a browser (fixtures). On the worker's tree: `pnpm test` 5253 passed and 0 failed, `proof:phase5` 34/34, `proof:editor-interactions` 79/79, `audit:a11y` 0 findings, `audit:testids` 663 ids and 0 lost. Then merged: the explanation is cleared from its job row in the same database transaction as the decision (accepted, rejected, stale) and on project deletion, and erased from the database file and the write-ahead log (`Store.erasing`: SQLite `secure_delete`, then a checkpoint that truncates the log — without it the planted word was still in the `-wal` file; what a filesystem keeps of freed blocks is not promised); a deleted project's rows are zeroed the same way; a cancelled request never stored an answer (pinned); an invalid answer is stored as one of 13 categories with a fixed sentence, so the owner no longer sees which argument was wrong; a service error text stays (no network adapter exists here, so whether a real service quotes the request in an error is unchecked; a future adapter should pass status and code, not the body). A backup made while a proposal is pending holds the sentence and the proposed operations (tested, stated in ADR-0027; decide or cancel before backing up). Byte searches over every file of the data directory and of a later backup after accept, reject, stale and project deletion find none of the planted words; undo and redo of the accepted edit unchanged. A Dialog over one of the editor's own modals keeps one toast region and one status with the toast's time carried on (`ds/toastNested.test.ts`, a model over the real host stack, clock and markup, since unit tests have no DOM; no such nested path exists in the editor today). On the worker's tree: `pnpm test` 5307 passed and 0 failed, `proof:command-bar` 23 + 6, `proof:phase5` 34/34. Lead decision: titles and taps stay on the present path (the exact path would cost about 10 ms per faded layer per 1080p frame for half a level). Two findings recorded: a stroked title fades per draw (the stroke shows through the fill, by design in `compositor2d.ts`), up to 64 levels from a fade of the whole title — a look question for the owner; the punch-in smear is 3 levels, bias +0.49, brighter than the nominal mean of its four quarter-strength copies (4 × 64/255 sums to 1.004).

The editor facts that limit use: SVG and animated pictures are refused at import, alpha exists for stills only. *Models (4 GB card, local only)* — keep the present speech, cleanup, upscale, interpolation and matting models; tier 1 to download, 8.74 GB: SigLIP 2 base (clip search), SAM 2.1 tiny/small, BiRefNet_lite, Depth Anything 3 Small/Base, YuNet (framing only), DictaLM 3.0 1.7B and Gemma 4 E2B as GGUF for a local Hebrew assistant bake-off (llama.cpp server, MIT, JSON-schema output; Hebrew quality unmeasured), the Dicta vowel-pointing model, PANNs Cnn14; no music-generation, stem-separation, Hebrew forced-alignment or better translation model qualifies cleanly. - **The two unexplained library one-offs are explained; one was a server defect, fixed in two steps (2026-11-26; one `opus` worker, then the lead).** (A) *`libraryContracts` "blobs 4 vs 3":* the test read its "before" count while the waveforms of two earlier audio imports were still queued (an upload answers before its waveform is made); reproduced with a gate on `Derivatives.intern`; the test now reads its counts after `derivatives.idle()`. Product untouched. (B) *`proof:library` check 16, music 7 where 8 after a restart:* **a product defect in `apps/server/src/library/index.ts`** — every index build in a data directory used the same temp name; when a service was stopped mid-reindex and the next one started in the same process, the stopped build's cleanup removed the new build's temp file, the new build's swap failed, the error was swallowed, and the status said `ready` over the previous index, one row short. The worker's fix: a closed service removes no file, and a failed build is named in the status problems (the error code only; English only, like the other problem texts). **The worker's new restart test then failed 3 of 12 and 2 of 10 runs here on a loaded machine with `SQLITE_IOERR_DELETE_NOENT`: one shared name was left, SQLite's own `<tmp>-journal`.** Established by a probe: `journal_mode = OFF` is refused by this SQLite build (the pragma answers `delete`), so every build transaction made and deleted a journal file by name, and a stopped build's rollback deleted the next build's journal. Lead fix: the build's journal is kept in memory (`journal_mode = MEMORY`, checked) — no journal file at all; the restart test passed 25 of 25 after (load 2–4; the failures were at load about 6, so the runs are not like for like — the mechanism and the error code are the evidence). A real process exit cannot overlap two builds, so on the owner's computer this needed an in-process restart to happen; the swallowed build error was real in any case. `proof:library` 18/18 once on the worker's tree (before the lead's step); library and contract tests 57/57 here. **Open:** the status says `ready` with a named problem when a build fails (no new state); the problem text has no Hebrew.

The helper reported the models and audio downloads running detached (session status, not a log read).
- **First owner run of `fetch-gated.mjs` on the laptop (2026-11-25, output pasted by the owner):** with a real key, 6 of 8 files landed with the expected sizes — Stable Audio Open Small complete (`model.safetensors` 1,676,829,212 B, `model_config.json`, `LICENSE`), pyannote `embedding/pytorch_model.bin` and the two `plda` files; `config.yaml` and `segmentation/pytorch_model.bin` failed with a bare "fetch failed" (a connection error, cause not shown; other downloads were running on the machine). The six SHA-256 values are recorded in `tools/library/expected/gated-models-01.json` as first seen. The script now retries a failed connection four times and names the cause. A malformed paste was refused before anything was sent (seen once).
- **Second owner run (2026-11-25, output pasted by the owner): 8 of 8 gated files in place** under `E:\PULSEFRAME-Library\models\gated\`; the two missing files came down on the retry build, the six present ones re-hashed to the same values. All eight SHA-256 values are now recorded in `tools/library/expected/gated-models-01.json` (first seen, not the publisher's). **Checkpoint 19b — the UI redesign "Pulse" wave A merged; the life line (ADR-0021) with people; content packs, title fonts and built-in music (ADR-0022) built; proof repairs landed**
- **UI redesign, wave A (merged):** four builders (top bar, navigation and start hub; timeline; stage and inspector; catalogue, AI hub, captions and the export sheet), each reviewed on its screenshots and repaired; then a lead integration pass in three worktrees: a per-project timeline behaviour (`settings.timeline`, op `set_timeline_behaviour`; NEW projects have the main-track magnet and ripple delete on, a stored document without the field behaves as before) and op `ripple_delete_clips` (one transaction, exact undo, linked sound moves with its picture, a J-cut that would land before 0 is refused in Hebrew); the editor's wiring (export always opens the sheet, one sheet host, a ring chip that reopens any running export, cut selection and panel requests in the store, undo toasts that undo exactly the delete they report, draggable gallery tiles, AI cards in plain Hebrew); the stylesheet conflict that made menus see-through, `.pf-force-dark`, dead legacy rules removed, `theme.cascade.test.ts` rewritten for the new layout. Reviews found and fixed, among others: the "+" on a cut covering short clips, Hebrew roll arrows reversed, keyboard focus lost after every inspector change, the cut inspector deleting the clip instead of the cut, the copilot's "undo" reverting the owner's own edit, an environment-variable name shown to the owner. **Not established yet:** the end-to-end proofs on the new interface (a worker is migrating their selectors and running them now), the accessibility audit, the look at sizes other than the builders' screenshots, any person using it.
- **Life line (ADR-0021):** capture dates read from the original file at import (Apple creation date, EXIF with offset, container time in the home zone, file-name patterns as estimates; no location read or stored), the Hebrew calendar in our own integer arithmetic (0 mismatches against ICU over 1900–2100; published dates checked against one published calendar), events by time gap with an explicit before/after-sunset answer, live title fields ({date}, {hebrewDate}, {year}, {hebrewYear}, {event}) that render exactly like typed text, the date stamp from the capture time, "make it plain text", and a preflight blocker for a field without data. `pnpm proof:lifeline` 14/14 (two checks corrected, see the commit). **People** (owner-approved with conditions): names, optional birth dates and a word choice per person in the project file; {name:…} and {age:…} fields with Hebrew age wording; an agent without the people scope (the default) is served every surface from a document without birth dates, so it sees no birth date, word choice or computed age, and cannot export a film whose titles show an age; an archive option «בלי פרטי משפחה». Its proof checks 10 and 11 are written and not yet run.
- **Content packs (ADR-0022):** a pack is data; the Hanukkah pack (first) and the birthday pack with original elements drawn by formula, templates, looks, caption looks, text styles and titles; five more OFL Hebrew title families switched on after a glyph test (Suez One, David Libre, Amatic SC, Fredoka, Gveret Levin; Alef and Varela Round held: reserved font names); built-in original music from our own deterministic synthesiser (six styles, measured, **unheard**). The REST routes, agent tools and the UI mounts are being wired now; the pack, font and music proofs have not run yet.
- **Wave-5 wiring and polish (after 19b's first draft):** REST routes and agent tools for packs, pack suggestions (birthday hints only for a credential with the people scope) and built-in music; Hebrew wording for every new refusal; the people surface walk with real commits (109 REST routes, 162 agent tools, no birth date without the scope); `people: {}` reads as absent. Looking at the rendered stills found four faults, all fixed with a check each: two titles of the birthday invite overlapping (new check P13: no two titles on screen overlap, for every pack template), pack looks blurring the template's own graphics (the planner now puts a template's elements above its looks; the built-in quote card's divider had the same fault, so the template parity golden was re-recorded for the four built-in templates with both), «נר 3» drawn with the outline hanukkiah (an element id can now hold the night; a test holds words and drawing together for every night), pale candles and an oil jug that vanished on light backgrounds (redrawn). Fonts proof: Gveret Levin's under-points were judged against the bet's tail (bound corrected, control kept), Rubik's holam may sit up to 0.20 em left of the ink, the controls rebuilt as mutations of each face's own marks (33/33 reject), Karantina's smallest file is emitted instead of inlined. **Verified here:** `pnpm proof:music` 24/24, `pnpm proof:packs` 28/28, `pnpm proof:fonts` 11/11, `pnpm proof:lifeline` 17/17, `pnpm check:notices` (11 font families). **Not established:** anyone listening to the music (every style is labelled unheard); the owner's review of the Hebrew wording; an agent that calls add_adjustment directly still covers a pack's elements (only the pack page uses the ordering op).
- **Proofs migrated to the new interface (one worker, one proof at a time):** a shared helper `packages/evals/src/editor-ui.ts`; selectors moved to the new homes (mode buttons, format chips, export sheet, agent menu, card menus, rails); eight assertions changed in substance and each made at least as strict (listed in the merge commit and the worker's report, e.g. phase 3 checks what each mode shows per tab and two more modes; the accessibility audit also checks the export sheet as a dialog and four new states). Product faults it found and fixed: the export dialog dropped focus to the page when a step replaced the focused control (Escape stopped working); style tiles closed images still being drawn; covered ruler labels still read by screen readers; inputs without a focus ring; a switch read as unnamed; the gallery's animated tiles showed the bare background between frames (a flicker the flash guard cannot see; now drawn off screen). **Verified on that branch:** accessibility audit 0 findings (he, en; 32 states each), test-id inventory 0 lost, editor interactions 49/49, owner UI 9/9, Phase 0 55, 3 53 (baselines re-recorded once for the new layout), 4 22, 5 34, 6 37, 7 33, 8 21, 9 14, 11 12, 12 20, browser export 26, speed 19, pitch 12, GPU 92/92 (at load 2.9; 91/92 at load 8.5), time 26, overlays 40, sound effects 29+9+36, voice cleanup 11, colour match 17, tracking 26, adjust 11, templates 70, AI panel 15, trend transitions 15, light 15, text 25, life line 14, reverse sound 7. **Failing:** creative 40/41 — the gallery hover check needs at least two light rises and the loaded machine repainted the tile too rarely to show them; the safety half holds; not loosened. Recorded, not fixed: the marker card covers the start of the top lane while the playhead rests on a marker; "move track" between tracks of different kinds no longer changes the rows visibly; a mixed-language technical line in the AI installer detail.
- **Regression on the fully merged tree (19c), load 0.6–4:** owner UI 9, Phase 0 55, 3 53, 4 22, 5 34, 6 37, 7 33, 12 20, browser export 26, GPU 92, creative 41/41, templates 147, adjust 11, tracking 26, colour match 17, voice cleanup 11, life line 17, packs 28, fonts 11, music 24; then, after fixes, editor interactions 49/49 (the drawer check now expects the built-ins, each pack's templates and the two gated ones, all taken from core), trend-text 25/25 (it picked "the last ten styles", which became the packs'), test-id inventory pass (text-font → font-option, recorded), lifeline-ui 27 checks (first run; eight faults in the proof found by running it, each rewritten to measure what it claims), accessibility audit 0 findings over 60 states (new: life library by date and by event, field menu, people sheet, field error, pack page, music browser, title fonts); unit tests 3056. Product faults found by these runs and fixed: Ctrl+Z did nothing while a radio, checkbox or slider had the focus; a dialog took a child's focused field as its opener, so closing the people sheet dropped the focus; the birth-date field had no focus ring; event names in the media drawer were squeezed to one letter per line. Seen in screenshots and not yet fixed: the inspector header shows a raw {date}; the music clip label is English in the Hebrew interface; the pack page's six tabs are cramped; a template hint shows «נר {night}»; one fix button in the export sheet is English.
- **Flash safety on screen made whole again (ADR-0009 amendment, 2026-11-18):** the GPU shuttle check was made strict (margin of need, a floor that grows with the window, wrong-gap guards replayed and required to fail, a `too-close` verdict; both mutations — no guard, a 0.25 s gap — fail it), and it then found a real hole: the guard counted what it decided rather than what was painted, and stamped its time before the draw (four rises in 0.9 s in one run of eight). Fixed with two more holes found on the way (only the strongest light was followed; a weak first step set a rise's time too early). `pnpm proof:gpu` 92/92 twice, trend-light 15/15, creative 41/41, unit tests 3092.
- **Keyframe curves and motion paths (ADR-0019) and multicam (ADR-0020), wave 4, built and merged (checkpoint 19c):** six builders, each reviewed and repaired, then two integration agents. Curves: named curves beyond the five v1 easings, custom cubic béziers, spring, bounce and smooth (Steffen) keys; a key inserted inside a curved segment keeps its shape (de Casteljau split); turn-back curves are refused on opacity and on every effect parameter that changes light (flash safety); a keyframe strip under the selected clip with a curve picker (Alt+K, Enter, Escape returns focus), and a motion path on the stage. Multicam: groups of angles synced by sound (coarse/fine with honest verdicts — confident, ambiguous, inconsistent — and capture dates pre-aligning a family event's phones), a program placed on new tracks, live switching with digits 1–9, an angle viewer that follows the reading direction, an auto-switch draft that follows who speaks, and «סנכרון וחיתוך» that stops for review when an angle is not confident; privacy regions follow each angle's own source. **Verified here (one run each):** `pnpm baseline:keys --check` 0 differences (old projects sample exactly as before), `pnpm proof:multicam-ui` 15/15, editor interactions 58/58, accessibility 0 findings in Hebrew and in English (five new states), test-id inventory 0 lost (567 → 601), `pnpm conformance:render-fuzz` 2/2, `pnpm proof:phase3` 53/53 (baselines re-recorded after comparing the screenshots: the new Shorts tab and media card), `pnpm proof:keyframes` 14/16, `pnpm proof:multicam` 18/19 (the 60-minute M3 run was not done: about 1.4 GB of temporary space), parity Test 1 exact, unit suite 4194 passing (two music timing tests fail under load and pass alone). Product faults found by the integration and fixed: the strip's S/L keys set one side only; a negative number could not be typed in the curve picker; the spring curve used `Math.exp`/`Math.cos`, which differ between Node and Chromium (now plain arithmetic, all new curves bit-identical across hosts); the auto-switch split a speaker's turn at syllable gaps and cut to a camera before it had picture; a camera paused mid-recording was synced with a wrong offset (now 'inconsistent'); two files of one camera overlapping by a sample refused the whole sync; the angle viewer ran left to right in Hebrew; the live report called landed cuts lost. **Closed after two rounds of lead decisions:** `pnpm proof:keyframes` 15/15 — every new curve bit-identical between Node and Chromium; the five v1 easings are not re-baselined, their easing shape differs by at most 0.5·2⁻⁵² between the two hosts (V8 `**`), which is gated (240 of 34,864 shapes differ; the value-level figure, 2.0× one ulp of the larger key, and a per-sample histogram stay in the evidence); the 2000-key commit, 31 ms against +15 ms (one sample), is recorded, not gated — the cost is per commit and grows with the document. `pnpm proof:multicam` 19/19 — overlaps went wide 0.21 → 0.50 (both voices above their own threshold for 0.5 s go wide) → 0.91 (the planner looks ahead: no single-speaker cut when an overlap starts within the 2 s minimum shot, which stays); 49 of 185 overlap frames the fixture marks have at most one voice above its threshold and stay counted as misses. `pnpm conformance:parity` — Test 2 now compares the export with the compositor frame sent through the same BT.709 4:2:0 round trip (precedent: life-line check 4) at the unchanged 36 dB: 41.71 dB; the full-chroma figure 35.73 dB and the controls (4:4:4 lossless 50.78 dB, 4:2:0 lossless 36.62 dB) are reported beside it. Also fixed: a one-frame flash of another angle where a camera's second file starts a fraction of a frame after the first ends; the media card's pick box over the format badge; the people-surface walk read raw tar headers (a checksum spelled «2014») — it now reads the archive file by file, with planted-year controls that must fail. **Not verified:** real footage from several phones, a real family event, the laptop's GPU.
- **Perform mode (ADR-0025), contracts merged:** the workspace «הופעה» (Shift+7); a take is planned from raw taps by pure core functions and committed once as the internal op `apply_take` through `POST /takes` (or the agent tool `perform_take`): one transaction, one undo, recorded as a run. One flash rule with a frozen table (a later reclassification of effects ships as a new op version); a take changes light only on clips it adds, works only on its own sequence, adds no titles with live fields, and the mode bar is ignored while a take is recorded (all found in review or by the lead and fixed). Camera takes are partial retakes over their range. Until the owner calibrates the taps on the laptop the view says «לא כויל» and never calls a take in sync. Three builders running (planner, camera path, perform view).
- **ADR-0018…0024 and ADR-0026 written** from the plans, the lead's decisions and the builders' reports.
- **Perform mode (ADR-0025) integrated and proven (2026-11-18):** the three builders merged with the core fixes (camera retakes hold the framing outside their range, panoramas filmed with a note, the spring at exactly settleSeconds, envelopes that do not fit held, a punch stopped where a higher picture begins). `pnpm proof:perform` 28/28 (docs/evidence/perform-proof.json): quantise 64/64 in the browser and on the server for beat, half beat, ÷2, ×2 over the analysed 120 BPM loop; flash — two colour pads at 20 taps a second pass the export's flash check and fail it with the rule switched off (control), and the same with the strobe pad on the software WebGPU adapter; the punch zoom 1.18 ± 0.02 on lossless stills, neighbouring frames byte-identical, the export's first zoomed frame is the hit frame; camera stills within 0.01 of the dense path (export 0.03), no background pixel, partial retakes keep every frame outside their range (also on a clip with no keys, on stills); one hash for the args in Node and the browser; undo/redo/replay hashes; nothing committed while playing, the sound session kept through 50 taps; keyboard wiring within 30 ms of the audio clock (latency NOT checked); preview equals stills pixel for pixel; sound hits within one frame. Found and fixed in the integration: the view covered the whole picture (now a row under the viewer), pads named by English ids in Hebrew, the camera overlay sent clip-local sample frames (a take on a clip not at 0 planned the wrong frames), the camera box overlapped its controls, the ghost banner had 1.68:1 contrast. The live path for 200 hits passed its one-frame budget in one run (slowest 29 ms) and missed it in another (35.5 ms): it sits at the edge on this machine; the cost is in apply_take's steps (rational comparisons, schema parsing, GC), not in the planner.
- **Family film maker (ADR-0026) and long video to shorts (ADR-0018, wave 4; schema version 3 with an identity migration), built and wired:** the film planner picks measured moments (motion, sharpness, sound; faces not used — no approved model — and the plan says so), orders by capture time, by hand or as an energy arc, cuts on the music's measured bars no closer than the flash gap, explains every pick in Hebrew and labels the whole a heuristic draft; one apply is one transaction; regeneration keeps hand edits, kept clips and a hand-set gain; an agent without the people scope is refused any title with an age. Shorts: every short is its own sequence reached through one projection; privacy regions of the source follow into each short (found in review — they were dropped); score shown as relative heuristic; the export sheet exports the active short (found in review — it exported the main edit). **Verified here:** `pnpm proof:family-film` 41/41 after a fix in the measurement (F9b first failed: an audio pre-echo made the shoulder of one activity a second moment; a second moment must now be a distinct activity, moments algorithm 2, so older measurements are measured again; the fixture moved to 16 December 2025, 26 Kislev 5786, checked against the project's calendar and Intl, and its dates are now read on upload; one load-related flake of F18 seen once), `pnpm proof:shorts` 48/48, editor interactions 56/56 (new: keyboard focus on the new short's tab and to the main tab), Phase 0 55, Phase 12 20, browser export 26, life line 17, test-id inventory 0 lost, accessibility 0 findings, 3876 unit tests. Product faults found by the wiring and fixed: film reasons naming a moment the clip does not show, a silent black gap after regeneration (now warned), the own-sound track lost on regeneration (hand gain lost), sequence-tab arrows counting from the active tab, tabs without aria-controls. **Not verified:** real footage, the owner listening or looking; 
- **Phone drop (ADR-0023) and fast export (ADR-0024), built and wired:** a second plain-HTTP listener opens only while an owner-issued code is live, on the private address the owner confirms; one code per phone, many files, closes after 10 idle minutes; token, PIN and session compared as digests in constant time, never logged; per-device connection and rate limits (a slowloris hole found in review and fixed); one import queue for desktop, folder and phone imports with progress, cancel, retry and recovery after a kill (20 clips imported exactly once after SIGKILL). The fast export re-renders only changed segments with keys from everything that affects their frames and sound; a privacy-region change purges the project's segments at once, also for an export already running. **Verified here:** `pnpm proof:phone-drop` 31 pass, 0 fail, 2 blocked (P4b now reads the QR as shown on screen with an independent decoder, jsQR 1.4.0 test-only, installed after the owner approved it; still blocked: editor key timing during a conversion, a full-disk seam); later fixes: uploads of unknown size count toward the storage ceiling as they arrive, and an unclaimed device's connections have a 30 s lifetime and a bound on unread answers; `pnpm proof:incremental` 31/32 — I2 first failed (worst frame 37.87 dB against the 38 dB floor) and was fixed in the product, not the check: segments now make the single pass's coding decisions at scene cuts, at the frame before a join (an extra padded frame behind a forced keyframe, cut off afterwards) and at a segment's first keyframe; on the merged tree `pnpm proof:incremental` 32/32 (worst frame 38.35 dB, size ratio 1.028 — the margin is small because the single pass itself is 37.66 dB against the source at that frame); I12 A/V offset 0.063 ms, incremental 27.1 s against 216.3 s clean; Phase 0 55, Phase 4 22, browser export 26, editor interactions 49, test-id inventory 0 lost, accessibility 0 findings, 3526 unit tests. Product faults found by these runs and fixed: picked files lost by an early reset, a cache above a lowered cap, the sound mix exceeding the storage ceiling, lane and sheet planning different qualities. **Not verified:** a real phone (iOS, Android, HEIC), a camera reading the QR, a real home network, the Windows firewall prompt, the laptop's ffmpeg, a real full disk.
